### M7-T10 AI and traffic around the station

- **Accepted (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** the docking doors moved into T25 (accepted) and the freighter scene into T30 (accepted with T34's pods).

- **Goal:** AC9 apart from the bots.
- **Files:** the native avoidance and pursuit against the station's surface, the encounter director's raids at the station and
  civilian traffic through the aperture (Q8), allies launching from the bays (T08's sequence for AI ships), tests.
- **Cargo traffic (maintainer, 2026-11-13, Q8 refined):** Saturn freighters never enter: each holds at T05's hold point, detaches
  its cargo pods (M5's modular Saturns), and the pods are moved into the cargo bay between the bow's forks, then
  leave the world inside it; the freighter waits and leaves (or takes pods back out). **Freighters back in** (maintainer, 2026-11-13): a
  Saturn approaches the hold point stern first, its pods toward the cargo bay and its nose away from the station, and leaves
  nose first. **No loader ships** (maintainer, 2026-11-13: the
  arms and clamps in the cargo bay capture belong to a cargo loader ship, and loaders are skipped): the pods fly into the bay
  by themselves. **Two bays** (maintainer, 2026-11-13): the **main docking bay** (through the docking aperture
  into the spinning drum, its tunnel turning with the drum) and the **cargo bay** (the zero-gravity bay, on the stationary spine between the
  bow's two forks). A ship flying into the main docking bay matches the drum's roll (0.148 rad/s) before it enters the
  tunnel, so it does not scrape the turning walls. Ships that fit the docking tunnel (94 × 34 m)
  fly in through the aperture and leave the world at the inner airlock.
- **Allies from the bays (Q3):** up to 48 Starfuries: four wings (Alpha, Beta, Delta, Zeta), each launching from its own arm in
  two waves of six through T08's sequence.
- **From T06 (2026-11-13):** the AI picks the station's rows by centre distance (the nearest 12 within 5 km), so in a crowd a
  fighter can miss the station as a contact and not avoid it; the station's surface must always reach avoidance (AC9).
- **Raid targeting (maintainer, 2026-11-13):** raiders prioritise whatever is firing at them, station allies or the station;
  when they go for the station, its **weapon systems** (the grid's mounts, which can be knocked out) are their first targets.
- **Done when:** AC9's AI and traffic.
- **Done (as built; contracts in `docs/architecture.md` §20.4; awaiting the maintainer's live run):**
  - **The station always reaches avoidance (T06's open point).** A big hull whose skin is within 2 km of a ship is one of its
    contacts whatever the nearest-12 cut kept (`brain_behaviours::SURFACE_SIGHT_M`; `Field.surface_rows`), so the fire lane,
    avoidance and the attack always see it. Test: 16 raiders in a pack, cut at the nearest 2 and at 12.
  - **Why raiders stalled beside the hull (T07's finding), and the fix.** Three causes: an attack on the station flew at its
    centre, 300 m inside the hull; avoidance only cancelled a wish into the skin, so a ship whose target lay beyond the hull
    hung beside it; and a dive at 60 m/s could not be stopped by the side thrusters, which is all the dodge used. Now
    (`kernels::pilot::avoid`, `surface.rs`): within 60 m of the keep-off distance a wish into the skin is **turned along it**;
    a ship falling at the skin faster than a quarter turn and its retros can stop **pulls out** (nose to the burn, every
    thruster, guns held); a **turning hull is kept off where its parts sweep** (each volume's ring about the drum's axis, from
    its hull's points), not where they are this tick; the limits of two hulls are applied together, the nearer last. And an
    attack whose straight way is through a hull **flies round the station's axis outside the girth of every hull in sight**
    (`brain_behaviours::detour`), so nothing enters the 50 m slot between the spine and the drum, where the reactive rules
    alone still squeezed ships into the drum.
  - **Raid targeting (maintainer).** At a hostile big hull the ship's last attacker (inside M3's 10 s memory) outranks every
    other target: a fighter, or the station itself when the grid hit it (the drum's guns name the station:
    `Targetable.SPEAKS_FOR`). A run on the station aims at **the nearest working grid mount** (interceptors too), kept while
    it works, then the next, then the nearest skin; a mount on the drum is led with its skin's speed; such a run is a gun
    run, never a slashing pass. The mounts reach the brains from the launch's copy of the turret rows
    (`PickJob.points` → `Field.sub_points`). Away from any big hull the pick is M5's, bit for bit.
  - **Balance (D75), measured again with the raid at the station itself, no decoy:** 12 raiders from 0.9 to 1.7 km over L9,
    objective `BABYLON 5`: seed 1 all destroyed at 97.5 s, seed 2 at 110.2 s, seed 3 at 113.1 s, **mean 106.9 s** (band 90 to
    120, each seed 75 to 140); station lost 426 to 522 (worst section 8 to 10 %), one mount knocked out in every seed, no
    raider rammed the station, 1.8 to 2.5 % of the raid's flying time under 8 m/s. **No retune.** The capital is unchanged
    (134.3, 134.5, 134.7 s). The test now asserts no ram and under 5 % hanging.
  - **Docking lanes (native, `Route.lane`, `ScsBrains.set_route_lane`).** A route flown along its legs with every thruster,
    the sideways error steered out, slowing into a waypoint that holds, arriving within 15 m; per waypoint a nose direction
    (kept on the way and in the hold; a ship turns where it is before it flies such a leg) and a turn rate to match about
    the nose, with the hull's top brought to the turning hull's own. A lane is trusted against the big hulls (it leads
    through their openings); ships and rocks are still dodged.
  - **Traffic** (`StationTraffic`, Main's child; `DockingPlan`, pure; `FreighterPods`):
    - **Main docking bay:** a Skylark, Narn transport or Brezebel (the hulls that fit 94 x 34 m) flies the lane on the
      drum's axis, matches the drum's roll from the approach's near point (measured 0.141 to 0.155 rad/s against 0.148 from
      120 m outside the mouth, 0.2 m off the axis, lying square in the tunnel: a 40 m span does not pass it on its side), and
      goes back to its pool 50 m short of the inner airlock's wall. Undocking starts there, turning with the drum, and flies
      the lane out.
    - **Cargo bay:** a Saturn 3 flies to the lane at 20 m/s, stops 700 m short of the hold point, turns its stern to the
      bay there, backs in to the hold point (nose away within 0.9 over the last 300 m, inside 20 m of the point), its
      **three pods fly into the cargo bay by themselves** along `cargo_bay_path` (7 s apart, 22 m/s) and leave the world at
      the entry, it waits 75 s and leaves nose first. The Saturn's hull is one mesh, so while unloaded its hull is hidden and
      its break-up chunks stand in (bow and engine ride the ship, the pod chunks fly). The pods in flight are ambient (no
      body, target or row) and the Saturn's mass and hit volumes stay as they were. T11's ferry bots are not started with
      it (`bots cargo` still shows them; say if they should accompany every cycle).
    - **Through the gate:** arrivals come out of the scene's jumpgate and cross to the lane at 120 m/s, departures leave
      through it; with no gate they start 2.5 km beyond the lane and are let go 12 km out.
    - **Raids and defenders:** `StationTraffic.raid(n)` (console, `MissionApi.raid_station`) sends raiders with the station
      as objective and scrambles the wings in `data/station/traffic.json` (Alpha). **`launch wing` ships are a wing now**
      (`LaunchDirector._station_wing`): twelve strong, the first its leader, engaging at will, the others on its slots
      (they form on the leader with nothing in reach and rally on a wingmate in a fight); a wing name the player leads is
      left to the player.
    - **Light traffic by itself** while free-flight encounters are on: every 150 s one of dock 0.35, undock 0.25,
      freighter 0.25 (one at a time), raid 0.15 (4 to 6 raiders), at most 8 traffic ships, each a hash of the session seed
      and the event's index.
  - **Console:** command and provider `traffic` (`status`, `dock [near]`, `undock`, `freighter [near]`, `raid [n]`,
    `scramble <wing>`, `auto on|off`, `clear`), in `info.md`.
  - **Threads and parity.** Every per-tick decision above is in the AI job on the frozen rows; threaded against delayed and
    one thread against six give the same rows, commands and turret digests (`test_station_threads`,
    `test_station_grid_threads`, with raiders detouring round the drum). **No parity recording moved**: every new rule needs
    a big hull among the contacts, a grid mount or a lane, and the recordings have none.
  - **Tests:** Rust 173 (+11: the contact in a crowd, attacker first, not away from a hull, mounts first, a drum mount's
    lead, the lane's roll, backing in and turning first, the slide, the pull-out, the swept rings, an arm coming round). GUT
    `test_station_ai` (2: sixteen raiders round the spine to their targets with no ram and under 5 % hanging; a raid's first
    worn mount), `test_station_traffic` (4, on the main scene: docking with the roll matched, undocking, the freighter's
    cycle with its pods, a scrambled wing with orders engaging a raid), `test_docking_plan` (4), `test_station_grid_balance`
    (2, the raid at the station).
  - **Open (maintainer):** the ferry bots with a freighter cycle (above); section health and the mounts' health were left as
    T07 set them; a raid started inside the slot between the spine and the drum is not covered (ships are kept out of it,
    not led out); the Bokmai (30.9 m tall) is left out of the tunnel's hulls for want of room.

### M7-T11 Maintenance bots

- **Goal:** AC9's bots (D78, Q11).
- **Files:** `tools/procgen/assets/maintenance_bot.py` and its tests, `src/props/maintenance_bot.gd` and its loop path, tests.
- **Done when:** the bots fly and hide as Q11 says; their count and per-tick cost recorded.
- **Done (as built; approved by the maintainer 2026-11-13 after live runs, the loop bots and the cargo ferry with the backed-in Saturn 3; details in `docs/assets-station.md`, "Maintenance bots as built"):**
  - **Generator:** `maintenance_bot.py` (style `maintenance_bot.py`, metal finish): 2 348 triangles, 4.6 x 3.1 x 3.5 m, no collision,
    from `references/maintenance bot.webp` (shells with bands and a 9, truss, sensor head with a green lens, two three-segment arms with
    claws, thrusters). Python tests `test_maintenance_bot.py` (12: contract, budget, frame, metal maps, glow, determinism).
  - **Bots** (`src/props/`): `BotLoop` (closed Catmull-Rom path by arc length), `BotMotion` (patrol, retreat along the loop to the
    nearest of two hatches, enter, hidden, exit; fixed acceleration, never faster than 9 m/s), `BotAlert` (events within 1.5 km, quiet
    for 60 s), `BotData` (reads `data/station/bot_points.json`), `MaintenanceBot` (the thin node) and `BotFleet` (the pool, the alarms and
    the console). **Eight bots** on eight loops: the four Cobra arms (drum frame, they ride it), the spine, the bow prong, the drum's
    green zone (drum) and the reactor. The station builds them (`Station.build`, three additive lines in `station.gd`: a `bots` var and
    the fleet after the grid); they hang from `frame_piece` and `drum_pivot`, so rebases and the drum's turn cost nothing.
  - **Fighting:** any hit the hit resolver reports, any shot of the grid's mounts (both at their World point) and the grid going weapons free
    (edge only: the latch never clears by itself) alarm the bots within 1.5 km of it (everyone for the latch); a bot goes on round its loop to
    a hatch, sinks 5.5 m into the hull and stays in at least 8 s and until 60 s without an alarm, then each comes out 3 s after the last.
  - **Ambient only:** no `Targetable`, `Damageable`, body or core row; tests check none exists under a bot.
  - **Cost (D78):** `bots.tick_us`: 46 to 120 us a physics tick for all eight loop bots (about 6 to 15 us a bot) and about 100 us on
    average with the five ferry bots loaded too (13 bots), under 1 % of a 60 Hz tick, measured in the running game beside the renderer, so
    they stay in GDScript; more than a few dozen would go native.
  - **Console:** `bots info|hide|show|goto <n> [m]` and the `bots` provider (`info.md`).
  - **Tests:** GUT `test_bot_loop` (6), `test_bot_motion` (8), `test_bot_alert` (5), `test_bot_data` (2), `test_bot_fleet` (13: loops, speeds,
    riding the drum, a rebase, hiding near a hit only, coming back after the quiet, staggered, `bots hide`/`show`, ambient),
    `test_bot_loops_clear_of_hull` (3: the loops and hatch legs keep 8 m from the drawn hull over a full turn of the drum; helper
    `bot_mesh_field.gd`), integration `test_bots_on_station` (6: on the real station: mesh, drum, rebases, a hit, the grid's latch, no target).
  - **Cargo scene (maintainer's request after the live run; `bots cargo [n|off|view]`):** a Saturn 3 and 5 (up to 12) ferry bots
    shuttling between its cargo pods and the cargo bay. **The Saturn backs in** (maintainer): it holds at `cargo_bay_hold` **stern toward the
    bay**, its nose away from the station and its pods nearest the bay (test: its forward points away from `cargo_bay_entry`), and **T10
    must fly freighters the same way** (T10's section carries the rule). It is a pooled `saturn_3_freighter` taken out of M5's ship pool
    (`ShipPool.acquire`, the way every civilian is spawned) with an `IdleBrain` holding the brake, so nothing flies it away;
    `BotCargoScene.place_freighter(brain)` takes any `PilotBrain` and `CargoFerryPlan` says where the hold point is and which way a
    freighter faces: that is the seam for T10. The bots (`BotFerryMotion`, routes from `CargoFerryPlan` read from the station's
    `cargo_bay` hardpoints and the Saturn's pod boxes) come out of the bay's back wall, fly out the throat and the slot on their own
    lane (the return is the other side, lower), stop 8 m behind a pod's aft face for 6 s, fly back and sink into the wall for 6 s;
    20 m/s cruise, 4 m/s^2; staggered round a trip (138 s) so some go out as others come back; they hide in the wall when fighting is
    near (a bot on its way out turns round on its route). Tests: `test_cargo_ferry_plan` (5), `test_bot_ferry_motion` (6),
    `test_bot_ferry_fleet` (4), `test_cargo_ferry_clearance` (3: 8 m from the drawn hull, 5 m from the Saturn, 12 lanes, out and back,
    leaving out the last 20 m at the bay's back wall where the machinery stands close), and in `test_bots_on_station` (8) the Saturn at the hold
    point with its forward pointing away from `cargo_bay_entry`, the staggered phases and `bots cargo off`. `bots cargo view` and `bayview` put the
    player beside the lane or behind the Saturn.
  - **Sheet (H):** `references/screenshots/M7-T11 bots/M7-T11_bots_sheet.png` (bots at 11 m by the arms, spine, bow, band and reactor, and
    from 40 and 60 m).

### M7-T12 Tour points and the station sandbox

- **Goal:** the base of M8's exterior tour.
- **Files:** `data/scenarios/station_showcase.json` (start in a bay, the station's traffic on), the tour shots keyed on the tour
  points (`CinematicShot`), a `tour` console command that plays them, `info.md`, a test that every tour point exists in the asset.
- **Done when:** the sandbox starts from the menu and the tour plays end to end.
- **Done (as built; approved by the maintainer 2026-11-14 after watching the tour live):**
  - **Sandbox:** `data/scenarios/station_showcase.json` (also in the New game list as "Babylon 5 station", in `settings.scenario`'s choices
    and `Main.SCENARIO_CHOICES`): the player starts on Alpha 3 with three wingmen, so the launch plays, then flies free. New scenario key
    `station: {"cargo_ferry": n}` (`ScenarioLoader._station`, 0 to 12) starts T11's cargo scene (the Saturn backed in at the cargo bay and
    n ferry bots) at load (`Main._start_station_traffic`); the maintenance bots are always on. T10's `StationTraffic` is a separate switch
    (`traffic auto on`, or free-flight encounters): `traffic freighter` while the ferry's Saturn sits at the hold point would put a second
    one there.
  - **The tour** (`data/tour/station_tour.json`, `TourBook` in `src/camera/tour_book.gd`, pure; `TourDirector` in `src/camera/tour_director.gd`,
    Main's child): seven stops in order, `bow`, `main_docking_bay`, `cobra_arm`, `defense_grid`, `drum`, `vanes`, `cargo_bay`, one 9 s shot
    each (never under the 1 s floor, enforced when the file loads), captioned in the lower letterbox bar. The reactor and the grid tower
    are not toured (maintainer, after the first sheet); their hardpoints stay. A stop is a tour hardpoint (`StationHardpoints.tour_points`)
    or a view of its own (`camera`, `subject`, `body`; the grid stop has no hardpoint); `shows` names the grid mounts a shot points out.
    Moves are five templates (`push_in`, `drift_right`, `drift_left`, `crane_up`, `pull_back`) written in fractions of the camera-to-subject
    distance, drawn per stop from a seeded shuffle bag (`LaunchCut.unit`), so a tour differs run to run and no move repeats before its pool
    has played. Shots name their actors `<stop>.stop` and `<stop>.subject` (`TourBook.pose_of`, placed from the station's pose every frame, so
    a drum stop rides the drum and a rebase moves nothing on screen); every key carries an `up` against its stop, so the arm is never sideways.
  - **Arms:** the `cobra_arm` stop (camera above and beside the arm looking along the six-door strip, the drum behind) and the `defense_grid`
    stop (the light turrets L1 and L2 on an arm's forward prong) are `arm: true`: `TourBook.arm_turn` turns the stop about the drum's axis
    onto the arm whose whole sweep over the shot, from when the stops before it have played, stays 20 degrees clear of the launch-blocked
    sectors (the spine over the drum), drawn from the seed among the fit ones; the caption names the wing.
  - **Console:** `tour`, `tour <stop>`, `tour stop`, `tour status`, `tour seed <n|off>` and the `tour` provider (`info.md`). Not while the
    player is still launching. A or Start ends the whole tour (a shot that stops before its end was cut: `TourDirector._on_shot_finished`).
  - **Clearance (maintainer: "the camera is clipping through the Cobra bay walls"):** `tests/unit/station_clearance.gd` reads the distance
    from a point to the drawn hull (the frame's and the drum's meshes and their props, the drum turned to its angle at that moment).
    `test_tour_book.gd` holds every tour shot, every arm choice and 24 drum angles to 2 m (closest approach: over 8 m); the new
    `test_launch_shot_clearance.gd` plays every launch variant, in the phase's own window, on launches through the pure `LaunchSequence`
    (alpha doors 1 to 6, beta, delta and zeta door 3, three drum angles) against the hull, props and the open door leaves, 2 m. It found five
    launch variants in `data/launch/cobra_launch.json` with the camera in a wall or leaf, all moved: `rack_wingman` (19 m aft of door 6 was
    past the hall's end wall, now z 10 and 9), `hold_inside` (the same, now z 9), `drop_low_pass` (1.7 m from an open leaf, x -18 and -17),
    `turn_side` (1.7 m from a hall prop, x 15), `exit_side` (inside a prop at the release point, mirrored to the fighter's clear side). It
    also found a fighter released at the edge of the blocked sector skimming the spine (0.9 m, 3 s out): that is the hold's, fixed in T08's
    new bullet.
  - **Tests:** `tests/unit/test_tour_book.gd` (15: the file, every stop exists in the station, 1 s shots, bad files refused, plan order, the
    shuffle bag, the subject in frame in every shot, the grid stop's mounts on screen in every shot, the arm choice clear of the spine for
    its whole shot, the strip in frame on every arm and upright, a drum stop rides the drum, a rebase, unknown actors, the clearance, the
    field's own sanity), `test_launch_shot_clearance.gd` (1), integration `test_station_tour.gd` (8: the start in a bay with the bots, the
    ferry and the Saturn; no tour from the rack; all seven stops end to end with the subject on screen at three moments of each and each
    held its whole shot; A or Start skips; one stop alone and `tour stop`; the arm shot upright and rebase-proof; the filmed arm clear of
    the spine and named in the caption; the grid's turrets on screen), `test_scenario_loader.gd` (+1), `test_front_end.gd` (the menu lists
    and starts it).
  - **Sheets (H, approved):** `references/screenshots/M7-T12 tour/M7-T12_tour_sheet.png` and `M7-T12_launch_fixes_sheet.png`
    (RX 6950 XT, Forward+, X11, 1 280 x 720). `run_sheet.sh` beside them rebuilds both in one window.

### M7-T13 Performance and the GPU check

- **Goal:** AC10.
- **Steps:** on the maintainer's GPU, the station in view from a bay, from 10 km and from 1 000 km (FPS, draw calls, VRAM,
  `state render`); the tick with three squadrons, the grid and a raid of 12; fixes for what does not fit, or a report to the
  maintainer with options.
- **Done when:** AC10's numbers in the Done notes.
- **Done (as measured, 2026-11-15; Status stays in progress while the open items below are with the maintainer):**
  - **How.** HEAD 52d76310 in a staged scratch copy (native library built from it, station assets from HEAD with T43's
    rebuild), RX 6950 XT, RADV, Forward+, `station_showcase`. Load 1.9 to 2.5 on 20 threads during the GPU runs, no other Godot
    holding a lock; the desktop's own clients (browser, editors, 5.7 GB in swap) kept running. The monitors are 60 Hz 3840x2160,
    so the compositor caps native Wayland at 60 fps even with `--disable-vsync`: the uncapped reads ran through XWayland
    (`--display-driver x11`, a `window` of the stated size; XWayland's own fullscreen gave a 6144x3456 surface, not used). GPU
    time is the renderer's `gpu_ms`. Views: **Cobra rack**, the player on Alpha 3's rack 0.7 s into `launch alpha 3` (the cut's
    rack shot inside the bay, `launch seed 1`); **Cobra late**, 1.7 s in (the turn and the door); **main bay out**, on the drum's axis
    50 m inside the main docking bay's mouth looking out along the frame; **main bay in**, 80 m in front of the mouth looking in at
    the doors (both placed by a scratch-only `station goto` point, not committed); **10 km** and **1 000 km**, `far view station`.
    Each read after 4 s in the view.
  - **1920x1080, vsync off** (fps / frame ms / GPU ms / draw calls / triangles), high set; low set in brackets where it differs:

    | View | fps | frame ms | GPU ms | draw calls | triangles |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | Cobra rack | 531 (500) | 1.9 | 0.82 (0.89) | 158 | 299 214 |
    | Cobra late | 591 (599) | 1.7 | 0.83 (0.81) | 110 | 181 938 |
    | main bay out | 528 (523) | 1.9 | 1.07 to 1.09 (1.06) | 93 | 129 310 |
    | main bay in | 453 (501) | 2.2 (2.0) | 1.33 to 1.44 (1.29 to 1.41) | 237 | 365 964 |
    | 10 km | 830 (817) | 1.2 | 0.58 to 0.59 (0.58 to 0.61) | 219 | 284 624 |
    | 1 000 km | 823 (829) | 1.2 | 0.49 to 0.50 | 66 | 104 932 |

  - **3840x2160, vsync off** (the display's resolution), high set; low in brackets:

    | View | fps | GPU ms | draw calls | triangles |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | Cobra rack | 512 (485) | 2.43 | 157 | 325 316 |
    | Cobra late | * | 2.29 (2.27) | 110 | 186 102 |
    | main bay out | 350 (351) | 2.44 to 2.86 (2.44 to 2.48) | 93 | 132 164 |
    | main bay in | 279 (278) | 3.30 to 3.40 (3.12 to 3.33) | 234 | 381 880 |
    | 10 km | 604 (615) | 1.21 to 1.24 (1.17 to 1.23) | 227 | 347 690 |
    | 1 000 km | 767 (745) | 0.98 (0.94 to 0.98) | 66 | 123 578 |

    \* The 4K screenshot taken 54 frames earlier stalls the main thread for about half a second and pulls that view's one-second
    average down (258 and 187 fps; 33 fps in the vsync run); a probe without screenshots held 60 fps at 4K through the whole launch.
  - **Vsync on** (native Wayland fullscreen 3840x2160, high): 60 fps in every view (59 to 60), GPU 3.6 to 5.0 ms near, 1.7 to
    2.1 ms at 10 and 1 000 km (more than the uncapped run's: the GPU clocks down while it waits), draw calls as above.
  - **VRAM.** In the renderer (`render.vram_mb`): 927 MB at 1920x1080, 1 314 MB at 3840x2160, the same in every view and both sets
    (switching live to low keeps the high maps loaded; a session started on low was not measured, since saving the setting would
    change the shared user settings). sysfs `card1`: desktop baseline 4.3 to 4.7 GB before launch, peaks 5.6 to 5.7 GB at 1080
    (+1.0 to 1.3 GB) and 5.9 to 6.3 GB at 4K (+1.5 to 1.9 GB).
  - **The tick** (recorded run, 1920x1080 window, vsync on, the GPU; `perf on` and a scratch-only per-tick recorder of the perf
    and core providers): `traffic raid 12` (which scrambles alpha), then `launch wing beta` and `launch wing delta` 2 s apart: 39
    allied Starfuries (three wings of 12 and the player's three wingmen), the player, 12 raiders, the grid weapons free.
    Heavy fighting 35 to 57 s (1 321 ticks; bolts in flight 102 on average, 192 at most; the grid fired 28 light and 6 heavy shots
    and 75 interceptor pulses, 53 projectiles shot down; 53 ships down to 41):

    | Stage | p50 ms | p95 ms | p99 ms | worst ms |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | tick | 2.94 | 5.09 | 6.80 | 17.50 |
    | AI drivers (native think) | 0.50 | 1.52 | 2.06 | 2.58 |
    | flight (ships, bolts, props, GDScript glue) | 1.04 | 1.73 | 2.19 | 3.49 |
    | resolvers (hit, ram) | 0.20 | 0.49 | 0.64 | 13.97 |
    | late (wings, waves) | 0.37 | 0.66 | 0.86 | 1.93 |
    | physics (Jolt, sync) | 0.45 | 0.89 | 1.26 | 1.50 |
    | core: AI job on its thread (`step_us`) | 0.40 | 0.95 | 1.27 | 2.27 |
    | core: brain batch call (`batch_us`) | 0.34 | 0.56 | 0.70 | 1.23 |
    | core: turret rows and interceptor sweeps (`turret_us`) | 0.03 | 0.18 | 0.26 | 0.39 |
    | core: cannons (`cannon_us`) | 0.06 | 0.21 | 0.30 | 0.42 |

    The join never waited (`step_wait_us` 0). Frames: 58 to 60 fps, GPU 0.71 ms median (1.13 p99), 77 draw calls. The 30 s
    before the raiders arrive: tick p50 3.18, p99 6.33, worst 9.17 ms. One tick over 16.7 ms in the fight. Headless, the same
    fight: p50 1.77, p99 3.67, worst 5.32 ms.
  - **Over budget, one-off ticks** (found with timers in the scratch copy, not committed): the first `traffic raid 12` 115 ms
    (the raider pool grown by 12 in `ShipPool.prepare`, 31 ms, plus the scrambled wing's 12 allies topped up in
    `LaunchDirector`, plus first use); each `launch wing` 27 to 29 ms (12 allies prepared, about 2.3 ms a ship); a second and third
    raid 38 and 48 ms (each scrambles a further 12 alpha allies while the first are still out: allies 39, 51, 63; raiders reused).
    The first hit of the session 17.5 ms: `ImpactFlash` builds its quad for the bolt's colour (9.4 ms). `LaunchDirector` says a
    console launch may hitch and a mission sizes its pools; the station scenarios ask for 3 allies and no raiders, so the station's
    own traffic (raids of 4 to 6 with alpha scrambled) hitches too.
  - **Determinism and comparison.** From the same copy: GUT (GUT_SLOW=1) `test_station_threads` 1/1, `test_station_grid_threads`
    1/1, `test_turret_threads` 3/3, `test_core_threads` 7/7, `test_flight_parity_fight` 1/1, `test_station_jolt_cost` 1/1; Rust
    `cargo test --release` 191 passed. `bench_quick.sh battle` 500 and 1 000 (load 3.3 and 4.0, T38's benches running beside it):
    tick median 5.56 / 6.01 and 7.21 / 6.75 ms, p99 7.93 / 9.28 and 11.71 / 9.78 ms, every budget held; M6 measured 5.10 and 6.31,
    p99 6.87 and 8.48 at load 0.6, so within what the load explains.
  - **Screenshots:** `references/screenshots/M7-T13 performance/{high,low}_{1080,4k}_{cobra_rack,cobra_late,main_bay_out,main_bay_in,10km,1000km}.png`
    and `high_full_wl_vsync_*.png`.
  - **Open (maintainer):** the pool-growth hitches. Options: (a) the station scenarios ask for the pools their traffic needs
    (`raider_fighter` 12, `starfury_ally` 15), moving the cost to the load; (b) `StationTraffic` prepares them at the start from
    `traffic.json` (largest raid, scrambled wings); (c) prepare a few ships a tick ahead (the launch's door ripple gives 0.25 to
    0.5 s; a raid's raiders still need theirs at once); (d) leave console launches hitching as designed. Separately, whether a
    second raid should scramble another full alpha wing while the first is still out (T10), and whether `ImpactFlash` should build
    the bolt colours' quads at load (17.5 ms once).
  - **Fix (maintainer's decisions 2026-11-15):** (1) **Pools built at load.** `StationTraffic.reserve_pools` (called by `Main` when the
    scenario's `station` has `"reserve_traffic": true`: `station_showcase` and `station_launch`) builds the largest raid's raiders
    (`MAX_RAID`, 12) and a full wing (`LaunchDirector.WING_SIZE` 12 per wing in `data/station/traffic.json`'s `scramble`) beside the
    allies already in play (15 allied Starfuries in total in the two scenarios), so no raid, scramble or `launch wing` grows a pool; a pool size
    the caller fixed (`raider_pool_size`, `ally_pool_size`, the benchmark's and the dry-pool tests') is left alone. Scenarios that only sit
    at the station (sandbox, free flight, dogfight) keep their own pools and still prepare on demand. `launch wing` now goes through
    `Main.ensure_free` (the grown ships are watched and heard, which `pool.prepare` skipped). (2) **Reuse the wing that is out.**
    `StationTraffic.scramble_for_raid`: a scrambled wing launching or at least half strong (`LaunchDirector.wing_strength`, 6 of 12)
    takes the raid at will and nothing launches; the next wing of the file's order goes only when the ones before it are gone or below
    half; with every wing spent the first is refilled with the Starfuries it lacks (`launch_wing(wing, count)`). Allies stay at
    15 over repeated raids (they were 39, 51, 63). (3) **Flashes warmed at load.** `ImpactFlash.warm` makes the quad of every colour in
    `assets/weapons/*.tres` (`WeaponSpec.known_bolt_colors`: the Starfury, raider, light and interceptor grid pulses, T43's green heavy
    bolt, both turrets) and keeps 16 spare flashes, each already holding a quad so the renderer's first use of the glow shader is paid at
    load; `MuzzleFlashPool.warm` makes the 24 lights a volley takes (the tints and the 9x heavy size are per flash, nothing to build).
    **Measured** (scratch copies of HEAD and of HEAD plus the fix, `station_showcase`, default pools; headless / windowed 960x540 on the
    RX 6950 XT, noisy, other agents running): load +70 ms headless (`traffic.reserved_ms` 52 to 57 ms windowed; the whole load 350 to
    420 ms headless, 715 to 750 ms windowed); first `traffic raid 12`: the command 30 ms to 3.2 ms headless (windowed 133 to 195 ms to 3.3 to
    4.8 ms), the worst of the next ticks 33 to 6 ms (windowed 145 to 213 ms to 6 to 13 ms); `launch wing beta`: command 26 ms to 5.3 ms,
    worst tick 29 to 8 ms (windowed 41 to 54 ms to 5 ms, tick 8 ms); first impact flash `create` 10 ms (windowed) to 0.04 ms. What is left: a
    second raid while the first twelve raiders are still alive builds twelve more raiders (about 19 ms), as the pool holds one raid.

- **Done (2026-11-15):** AC10 measured (4d7bac10) and the maintainer's three decisions on the spawn stalls built (b012ff5f: pools at load for the station scenarios, the wing out takes a new raid, flashes warmed). Pushed with 6472d504; CI green (run 43780325834).

### M7-T15 Starfury and raider surfaces from show references (texture detail low and high)

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-12):** M5-T40 gave the nine civilian hulls show-like surfaces in two sets, chosen by the ship
  texture detail setting (low: major seams only; high: plating in mixed-size rectangles with per-plate tone and sheen, joint
  grime, recessed hatches and ribs). The setting is meant for every ship, but the Starfury, the allies' Starfuries and the
  raider still carry one set and ignore it.
- **Agent:** Fable. **Depends on:** M5-T40 (the surface layout code in `tools/procgen/core/seams.py`, `parts/layouts.py`, the
  setting and the generic loader: a hull with both sets picks by the setting).
- **References** (gitignored, inspiration only, never traced or reused): `references/T40 seam references/` holds the
  maintainer's Starfury close-ups (`ea starfury top close up`, `ea starfury bottom close up`, `ea starfury close up`, the two
  paint schemes) and the raider ones (`raider close up`, `raider and wagon close up`, `raider carrier` and `raider battle
  wagon` close-ups for the raiders' construction).
- **What:** per hull, a written read of its surface off the references; layouts per part for low and high; an A/B/C option
  sheet on the GPU for the maintainer before the generator commits (their standing rule); the Starfury's liveries (M4-T30's
  wing liveries) and the raider's paint unchanged unless the maintainer says otherwise. Tests pin the layouts as T40's do
  (feature counts and positions, mirror symmetry, minimum plate size, determinism).
- **Done when:** both hulls have low and high sets that the setting switches, the maintainer approved the sheets (H), tests
  pass, and `docs/roadmap.md`'s T40 follow-up is marked done for these two hulls.
- **Done (as built):**
  - **Choice (maintainer, 2026-11-13):** option A for both hulls from the phase 1 sheets
    (`references/screenshots/M7-T15 surface sheets/M7-T15_options_{starfury,raider}.png`). Starfury: framed panels with the two
    bent channels and the oblong port on the upper decks, the cabin roof's spine, panels and ports, swept strakes on the lower
    decks, ring courses on the pods over a ribbed rear housing, tone 6 %. Raider: a trapezoid panel in each wing bay with a
    smaller one inside the outer two, louvres, a long hatch, large plates, tone 7 %; the belly's weathering, the raider's paint,
    the claws' tooth rings and the Starfury's liveries unchanged. The allies' Starfuries share the player's asset and both sets.
  - **Code:** `layouts.starfury()` and `layouts.raider(sweep, root chord, half span, tip chord, nacelle axis)` in
    `tools/procgen/parts/layouts.py` (B and C and the prototype `surface` build option removed). `starfury_aurora.LAYOUTS` and
    `raider_fighter.surface_layouts(spec)` dress the parts; both generators bake with the generic plating off and
    `DETAIL_LEVELS = {"low": MAJOR}`, so each writes `<asset>_{albedo,normal,orm}_low.png` beside the full maps and names them in
    every surface's `detail.low`. Generator versions: `starfury_aurora` 10, `raider_fighter` 5. The loader needed no change: the
    livery and glass shaders and the raider's paint already swap sets through `MeshAssets._watch_detail` (M5-T40).
  - **Tests:** `test_seams.py` pins both hulls' feature counts per level beside the civilians' (`PINNED`), their plates' least
    size, centre-part symmetry, and a `FighterLayoutTests` class (the low sets' positions, the Starfury's deck frame inside its
    outline, channels and strakes parallel to the leading edge, the raider's bays and panels, teeth, louvres, tones,
    determinism). `test_starfury_aurora.py` and `test_raider_fighter.py`: the low maps in the file list and every surface's
    `detail.low`, the drawn parts and their twins' mirrored layouts. GUT `tests/integration/test_fighter_texture_sets.gd`: the
    setting swaps both fighters' maps in place (livery, glass and paint materials), glow shared, one mesh.
  - **GPU check** (RX 6950 XT, Forward+): `references/screenshots/M7-T15 surface sheets/final/{starfury,raider}_{high,low}.png`,
    the same ship before and after `set settings.texture_detail low`.
  - **Open:** the maintainer's look at the as-built frames (H). T16 rebuilds the raider's shape to its STL and keeps these
    layouts on the new parts.

### M7-T16 Raider fighter to its STL at 90% fidelity

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-13):** "the raider model differs too much from the reference STL ... the panels leading the wing
  are really wrong". The raider was built in M2/M4 without an STL (M5's 90% rule covered the civilians only); the maintainer now
  extends the rule to it.
- **Agent:** Opus. **Depends on:** T15 (its raider surface sets, option A, are committed first; T16 keeps that reading on the new
  shape).
- **Reference** (gitignored, never committed): `references/Raider_Fighter_16m.stl`; `references/raider.jpg`,
  `references/raider ships and battle wagon.png`, `references/T40 seam references/raider close up.jpg` for details the STL
  lacks.
- **What:** measure the STL with `tools/refview` (scale, axes, sections), list the differences (the wing's leading-edge panels
  first), rebuild `tools/procgen/assets/raider_fighter.py`'s shape to the 90% rule (top, side and front silhouette IoU >= 0.90,
  L/W/H within 10%), with the hardpoints (guns, engines, lights, hit volumes) moved to match. T15's surface layouts (low and
  high, option A) follow the new parts; paint unchanged. A before/after sheet against the STL for the maintainer (H).
- **Watch:** the raider's size and hit volumes feed the balance fights (M5's Q31 turret fight, M4's battle fixtures); a change
  in its hit volumes may move `tests/fixtures/` recordings and balance numbers, which the agent reports rather than retunes.
- **Done when:** the compare passes the 90% rule, scores in `docs/assets-raider.md`; Python tests pin the shape; the GUT files
  touching the raider and the parity recordings pass (or a recording's change is explained and approved); the maintainer has
  seen the sheet.
- **Done (as built):**
  - **The model.** `Raider_Fighter_16m.stl` (8 381 triangles) at 16 m across (its name; 0.12446 m per unit): 7.37 m long, 1.53 m
    high, mapped `--right=-x --up=+z --aft=+y`, the apex forward (the mood shot's glows trail the straight edge).
  - **What differed, the leading-edge panels first** (`docs/assets-raider.md`, "Fidelity"): the old wing's raised plates, vents
    and ochre band stood up to 0.4 m ahead of its 56 degree leading edge, a staircase in the top view; the model's edge is one
    47.8 degree line from the nose to a pointed tip, notched 2.7 to 3.4 m out for two short guns a side, the trailing edge
    straight and chamfered at the root; its body is a narrow ridge and keel between two flat engine housings across the wing, not
    a hump with two drums on top; small exhausts in the housings' backs; the canopy along the ridge over the nose.
  - **Rebuilt** (`parts/raider.py`: `RaiderWing`, `RaiderRidge`, `RaiderLobe`; `raider_fighter.py` generator 6): IoU top 0.605 →
    0.992, side 0.513 → 0.982, front 0.506 → 0.969; L/W/H 0.976 / 1.062 / 1.316 → 1.000 / 1.000 / 0.992. Every detail part sits
    0.1 m or more behind the leading edge (tested). Hardpoints moved: both guns of a side in its notch (muzzle names kept), the
    pods' nozzles in the housings' backs on the wing plane (the thrust line and centre of mass at y = -0.036), boxes for the
    ridge and housings, five 1.6 m wing steps; nav lights, RCS and the eye follow. `layouts.raider(...)` refitted to the new parts
    (option A's reading kept).
  - **Metal, not plastic** (the maintainer, added to T16): metallic and roughness per material (paint 0.45 / 0.50, panels 0.50 /
    0.46, the housings' bare `hull_metal` 0.72 / 0.34, grilles 0.75 / 0.36, barrels 0.90 / 0.26, nozzles 0.90 / 0.28), and
    `textures.metal_finish` (opt-in by `style.extra["metal_finish"]`, shared with T04 and T17): roughness +-0.12 per 0.9 m panel
    and +-0.06 drift, dirt up to 0.3 rougher and half as metallic; plates' sheen +-0.12 in the high set. Both sets; the albedo,
    normal and emissive maps byte-identical to the shape-only build, only the ORM maps change.
  - **The tier refit** (`test_tier_outcomes` failed: the model's to-hit 0.118 against full fidelity's 0.153): no stale size;

### M7-T17 The Starfury looks metallic, not plastic

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-13):** "pay attention to reflection and material appearance. We want the station to look metallic,
  not plastic", then "add the metallic not plastic instruction for both the Starfury and raider". The station's share is in T04
  (D72), the raider's in T16.
- **What:** real metallic and roughness maps for both texture-detail sets: painted plating reads as painted metal (moderate
  metallic, roughness varied per plate and panel), bare metal on the claws, gun barrels, engine pods' housings and nozzles
  (higher metallic, lower roughness), rougher grime and wear; a normal map from T15's option-A panel layout. Liveries and colours
  unchanged. A before and after GPU sheet with a sunlit grazing view, shown to the maintainer before the generator commits.
- **Done when:** the maintainer approves the sheet (H); tests pin the material values; both sets switch as before.
- **Done (as built):**
  - **Approved (maintainer, 2026-11-13):** the sheet as is,
    `references/screenshots/M7-T17 starfury metal/M7-T17_starfury_metal_sheet.png` (before v10 and after v11; high and low sets; a
    grazing sun over the decks, the three-quarter view, a close view at 7 m; the alpha livery; RX 6950 XT, Forward+, the game's
    lighting unchanged).
  - **Code:** the Starfury bakes with `EARTHFORCE_FIGHTER` (`styles/earthforce.py`): `EARTHFORCE`'s colours with a metallic and
    roughness per material (paint 0.45 / 0.48, panels 0.40 / 0.55, dark 0.60 / 0.45, frame 0.75 / 0.30, bells 0.90 / 0.28, barrels
    0.85 / 0.26, RCS 0.80 / 0.32) and two bare materials in the paint's colours (`ef_bare` 0.80 / 0.30, `ef_bare_trim` 0.75 /
    0.32) that `starfury_aurora.bare_metal` puts on the claws and the pods' rear housings, outside the liveries. The roughness drift
    and the dirt use T16's shared `textures.metal_finish`, with three keys added for the Starfury (`chip_dirt`, `seam_dirt`,
    `dirt_on_paint_only`; off by default, so the raider and the station are unchanged). The livery shader's clear coat lies on the
    paint only and follows its roughness. Albedo and normal maps unchanged; generator version 11. The nav buoy keeps `EARTHFORCE`.
  - **Tests:** `test_starfury_aurora.py` pins the values and colours, the bare faces, no livery on bare metal, both sets' ORM spread
    and the Starfury's `metal_finish` parameters; the GUT texture-set, livery, textured-hull, break-up and mesh-asset files pass.
  - **Sheet script:** `scripts/starfury_metal_sheet.sh` (one run per scratch project and prefix; X11, `godot_lock.sh read --gpu`).
  - **Reflections:** the key star's highlight, the white ambient (diffuse only) and, with `settings.hull_sheen` above 0, the nebula
    as sky radiance; no probes. Bare metal in shadow reads dark gunmetal under that lighting, which the maintainer kept as it is.

### M7-T18 Station exterior lighting

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-13):** "add a milestone task for exterior lighting", with three references. It fills out Q10 (running
  lights, beacons, lit windows, bay lights, windows on the night side) beyond the first-pass emissive windows of T04.
- **References** (gitignored, inspiration only, never traced or committed): `references/station lighting references/`
  - `station bow lights show capture.jpg` (show CG, bow three-quarter): the docking aperture's cross door lit red at its centre;
    short white light dashes in a ring round the docking sphere's rim and along the bands; the hull mostly dark under a rim light.
  - `station side lights fan render kosonen 2023.jpg` (a fan render, Kari Kosonen 2023; its look only, nothing copied): white
    dashed ring lights round every band edge and the sphere's rims; red beacons at the top and bottom tip of every vane and at
    the bow spike's tip; small lit windows in rows on the drum; the red cargo modules glowing; yellow "5" markings lit on the drum,
    the spine and the bow.
  - `cobra arms nav lights show capture.png` (show capture, the Cobra arms at the docking sphere): a ring of pink-red navigation
    lights round the band's edge next to the arms; white dash lights along the arms; the arms' faces lit warm from their bays.
- **The docking bay's red light (maintainer, 2026-11-13).** The entrance bay T05 builds is lit deep red from inside, as
  `references/docking bay references/` shows: the stepped tunnel's receding frames glowing red layer by layer, machinery walls in
  red with darker gaps, small red lamps in the mouth, pink-lit vertical panels at the mouth's edge, and the inner airlock's
  sawtooth doors in red. From outside the mouth reads as a red glow at the centre of the bow disc (`4 bow on toothed ring and
  cross housing.png`, and the bow capture in `references/station lighting references/`). T18 lights it: emissive red on the
  tunnel's frames and walls, falling off with depth, and one or two real red lights inside the tunnel through `LightPicker` so a
  ship flying in is lit red. It turns with the drum.
- **Since this was written (2026-11-14):** T25 and T32 lit both bays as red-lit grey metal from lamps through the light slots
  (`BayLights`), with no self-lit rings, strips or bands; the maintainer approved that over the emissive tunnel described above, so
  the bays are done and T18 leaves them as they are. T24 set the drum's windows (one straight row of evenly spaced lights on a dark
  bar along each of the 8 stringers, no rings, no scattered dots), the sphere's collar ring of densely lit panels, a windowless grey
  spine and command section, and the station's colours; all approved, and T18 keeps them. The maintainer rejected window rings round
  the drum and polka-dot scatter, so any ring or dash light T18 proposes is shown on a first sheet before it is built out.
- **What:** the station's emissive layer (dashed ring strips, window rows, markings, the aperture's red centre, the bay glow) in
  its maps with the hardpoints T05 gives the lights; blinking beacons (vane tips, the bow spike's tip) and running lights as cheap
  shader or emissive animation driven by sim time, not one light node each; a few real lights where a fighter flies close
  (the bays, the aperture) through `LightPicker`'s 8 slots (D74), so the station never starves the fight's muzzle flashes; the
  lights on the drum turn with it; the night side under the planet's shadow (M6) still shows its windows and lights; readable as
  a point of light pattern from 10 km and as a dot from 1 000 km (M6's far representations).
- **Done when:** an A/B lighting sheet against the references on the GPU (from a bay, 1 km, 10 km, the night side) approved by the
  maintainer (H); `state render` numbers (fps, draw calls, light slots used) recorded; tests for the light hardpoints, the blink
  timing from sim time and the slot budget.
- **Done (as built; the final sheet awaits the maintainer):** the layout the maintainer approved round by round (sheets in
  `references/screenshots/T18 station lighting/`), built from the generator's light hardpoints; details in `docs/assets-station.md`
  "Exterior lights".
  - **Points:** 15 red beacons (the bow spike's tips, the 12 vane tips, the reactor's aft tip) fading together over a 4 s cycle of sim
    time (1 s dark, 1 s up, 1 s full, 1 s down); the pink-red navigation ring (28 round the forward body's flare, between the arms); 32 red-pink navigation lights on the
    Cobra arms' aft parts (four a flank, as the arms capture); the bays' 10 lamps. No running lights, no dashed rings, no lit
    markings, no red glow in the docking bay (the maintainer). The beacons' beat slowed to 3 s (lit 0.8 s), then to the 4 s fade, after
    the maintainer flew them live (2026-11-15).
  - **Floods (74):** the docking ring's eight narrow pools; the Cobra arms (from the forward body's flare, from the core onto their
    aft parts, the same mirrored onto their fore parts and heads, the sphere's rear face kept dark); the spine; the ring in front of
    the vanes; the biggest band's forward face and the hull ahead of it. In the hull shader, each section holding only the floods
    that reach it (at most 54 of the 64 it can hold); no light node, no light slot.
  - **Cost** (RX 6950 XT, Forward+, 3840 x 2160, `SHEET_VIEWS=t18final`, today = `station_lights off`): GPU from a Cobra bay 3.26 to
    4.39 ms, the docking ring 3.01 to 3.94, the arms 3.73 to 5.07 (the prototype, every flood on every pixel: +1.91), 1 km 3.30 to
    4.49, 10 km 1.72 to 1.61, the night side 3.27 to 4.38 and 2.85 to 3.68; draw calls +2 or +3; light slots in use unchanged (the
    key star and the bays' lamps). Sheet: `references/screenshots/T18 station lighting/T18_final_sheet.png`.
  - **Accuracy record** (not a gate; the maintainer judges by eye): the camera fitted to the two show captures matches their
    silhouettes at IoU 0.810 (bow) and 0.881 (arms); the lit regions overlap at 0.22 and 0.24. 0.90 cannot be reached: the show's
    model differs from ours (built to the STL), the captures' bright areas are mostly sunlight, and the approved paint and texture
    differ. Files: `references/screenshots/T18 station lighting/measure/`.
  - **Tests:** `test_station_hardpoints.py` (the counts, every light on its piece and every flood's beam on the station, the arms'
    nav lights red-pink, the sphere's rear face dark and the arms lit, the ring in pools with gaps), GUT `test_station_light_plan`
    (groups, floods, the blink from sim time, which floods reach a section, floods turning with the drum) and `test_station_lights`
    (the real station: counts, the shader's budget, inner faces without floods, the drum's floods carried round, no light node, the
    bays' lamps the only rig emitters, yielding every slot to muzzle flashes).

- **Fix (maintainer, 2026-11-13, before this task is built): flicker, the sun through the station, glints along the core.** Seen in
  the launch shots and from outside as the drum turned; approved live. Causes and fixes (details in `docs/assets-station.md`
  "No two faces in one plane" and "Solid to the eye, and the sun kept out"):
  - The halls' work lamps blinked: each lamp's box lay flush in its wall panel (z-fighting). Lamps stand 0.3 m proud; every
    other pair of faces in one plane in the props, the drum, the frame's detail and the hangar behind the menu moved apart
    (`test_coplanar_overlaps.py`). `LightPicker` was not involved: its slots held steady throughout.
  - The sun through the Cobra doors and light at their corners: the leaves did not meet and the lit strips did not reach the
    wells' walls or corners. Leaves meet, a lit sill closes each well; no ray leaves a hall with its doors closed
    (`test_station_sealed.py`).
  - Sun rays through the core: rings left open between the drum's shells. Each shell ends and starts in a flange; no line
    passes through the station.
  - Glints off the arms, teeth and decks on the night side, and sunlit halls: nothing cast a shadow. The key star casts the
    station's shadow (the station only; ships receive it), and what stands inside a hall or a bay is on an interior layer the
    star's light leaves out (`LightRig`). T18's real bay lights go through the 7 slots as planned and light that layer.
  - Ships inside are lit as interiors (maintainer, 2026-11-14): a fighter on its rack and rail, a ship in the docking tunnel
    or the cargo bay (`InteriorLight`, `InteriorZones`); `test_interior_light.gd` (3), `test_interior_ships.gd` (3).
  - Tools: `scripts/flicker_probe.sh` and `scripts/flicker_metric.py`. Sheets: `references/screenshots/M7 bay flicker/`
    (`outside_before_after.png`, `compare_heat_before_after.png`).

### M7-T19 Station sections and near-only detail

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-13):** "because of the huge size of Babylon 5, is it possible to apply the reduced fidelity for distant
  models logic to subsets of the model?" Godot picks one LOD per mesh instance from its distance to that instance's bounds, so a
  fighter 50 m from a bay draws all 8 km at full detail, and one 8 km mesh is almost never frustum culled.
- **Agent:** Opus. **Depends on:** T04 committed. **Before:** T05, whose detail is built on these sections and props.
- **What:**
  - The generator cuts the frame and the drum into sections along T01's section lines (about 10 to 20 per piece; long sections
    such as the green zone split further), each a mesh with its own origin and LOD chain. The manifest lists them per piece;
    Godot builds each as a child `MeshInstance3D` of the frame or drum node, so the drum's sections turn as one rigid body with
    no seams opening. The collision pieces (D73) follow the same sections.
  - Per section: automatic mesh LOD and a visibility range with fade, shadows off beyond a distance; sections out of view are
    culled. Section boundaries hidden at seams the hull already has (band edges, rings).
  - Near-only detail props: a `MultiMesh` path for repeated small parts (door frames, clamp arms, antennas, pipes, greebles),
    shown only within a near range (about 1 to 2 km, measured), as the seam T05 builds its detail on. A few T04 parts move to it
    as the proof.
  - Occlusion: occluders built in code inside the drum and the spine (`OccluderInstance3D` with an `ArrayOccluder3D`) if the
    measurement shows a gain; else noted for T13.
  - Far representations (M6) unchanged beyond their range.
- **Measure** on the GPU: from a Cobra bay, the aperture, 1 km, 10 km, 1 000 km, before and after: fps, GPU time, draw calls,
  triangles drawn, VRAM (`state render`). Record them in `docs/assets-station.md`.
- **Done when:** the sections and props in the manifest and in the game, the drum turning as one body, the STL compare still
  passing, the numbers recorded, tests (sections cover the piece without gaps, each section's LOD chain and range, the props'
  range, determinism), and no visible popping or seams on a fly-along sheet (H).
- **Done (as built; the fly-along sheet awaits the maintainer):**
  - **Sections:** the drum in 12 and the frame in 16 (`DRUM_PLANES`, `FRAME_PLANES` in `babylon5_station.py`, generator 2), cut on
    the hull's own joints (band edges, rings, the gaps between cargo modules and between vanes; the green zone at its blocks'
    joints). `core/sections.py` clips the packed piece's triangles exactly (positions, normals and UVs), so the sections tile the
    piece with no gap or overlap and draw on the piece's maps; each is `<piece>_sec_<name>.glb` with its origin on the axis, listed
    in `mesh.sections`. Godot: `PieceSections` (pure: reads and checks the list, the props' range) and `RenderPiece` (a
    `MeshInstance3D` per section under the drum's or frame's node, one material set per piece, `MeshAssets.section_mesh`); the
    drum's sections turn as one body (tested at a turned drum: every joint point lands at one place).
  - **Levels of detail:** the importer builds a chain per section, but does not hold a section's border still (measured: up to
    half a section's border vertices gone at its coarser levels), so each section has a 6 m **skirt** in its cut planes, facing out
    of it, that shows hull through any crack between neighbours at different levels (9 480 triangles).
  - **Ranges, shadows:** the hull's sections are drawn at every distance (they are the hull; M6's far prop still shrinks them and
    hands over to the dot); no light in the game casts shadows, so `cast_shadow` stays off (nothing to switch by distance).
  - **Near-only props:** `core/near_props.py` (kinds with their own atlas, groups of copies per section) and `parts/station_props.py`:
    the Cobra doors' faces (6.25 cm a texel: grated leaves, rivets, a hazard band, the bay's light between the slats, the number
    crisp) and the clamps (moved off the hull; their red caps painted on the rails as the far stand-in), instanced under the drum's
    `waist` section, shown within 1 500 m and faded over 400 m (where the hull atlas's 1.39 m texel reaches a pixel at 1 080 rows).
  - **Collision by section** (`parts/station_collision.py`, the manifest's `collision`): frusta, slab runs and boxes cut at the
    same planes, named `<section>/...`, each piece listing its sections' spans; drum 114 shapes, frame 128; gap at most 12.8 m and
    outer faces at most 58 m off the hull (the vanes' roots and notches). A first pass: T05 opens the aperture's tunnel and fits the
    bays.
  - **Measured** (RX 6950 XT, 6 144 x 3 456, `SHEET_VIEWS=t19 scripts/station_sheet.sh`; numbers in `docs/assets-station.md`): GPU
    time unchanged within 0.05 ms at every view (bay 3.92 ms, aperture 4.00, 1 km 4.00, 10 km 2.18, 1 000 km 1.76), draw calls
    15 to 18 → 41 to 44 near, triangles drawn up 5 to 15 % (the skirts), card VRAM unchanged within the spread (the prop atlas
    about 14 MB). The 40 k-triangle hull is not geometry bound at 6K, so the sections buy nothing yet; they are the seam T05's
    detail needs. **Occluders not built:** the station's geometry costs at most about 0.4 ms, so they are noted for T13 (ships
    behind the station in a fight).
  - **STL compare** still PASS (the clamps were the only shape removed; whole-piece glbs still written for the tools).
  - **Sheet (H):** `references/screenshots/M7-T19 station sections/M7-T19_station_sections_sheet.png` (the fly-along under the
    station from the bow to the reactor at about 200 m, the doors at 20, 50 and 300 m before and after).
  - **For T05 and T18:** see `docs/assets-station.md`, "Sections and near-only props".

### M7-T23 The jumpgate and jump points refined from the maintainer's GIF

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-13):** "I've updated the jumpgate reference folder with a gif to refine the jumpgate appearance."
  Raids and civilian traffic (T10) and M8's tutorial raiders arrive through the gate and through jump points, and the gate stands
  50 km from the station in the same scene.
- **Reference** (gitignored, inspiration only, never traced or committed): `references/jumpgate/Jump gate opening (arrival), jump
  point forming (with small pause), and jump gate jump to hyperspace.gif` (28.7 s; contact sheet in `references/screenshots/
  jumpgate gif frames/`): the gate's four arms light, a white flash fills the centre, then a feathery, swirling blue vortex with a
  deep dark eye opens and a ship comes out; a ship's own jump point starts as a small bright star, pauses, then opens into the same
  blue swirl, wide then funnel-shaped; a jump into hyperspace through the gate shows an amber-orange vortex and tunnel.
- **What:** M6's `jumpgate` asset and its vortex (M6-T19, T20) refined to the GIF: the arms' light-up sequence, the white flash,
  the vortex's feathered swirl, depth and eye, its opening and closing timing, and the jump point's spark, pause and opening, as
  data; distance detail as M6 built it (up to 100 jump points in a battle) kept within its GPU budget. The amber hyperspace side
  is recorded for M11 (Hyperspace), not built here.
- **Agent:** Opus (or Fable). **Depends on:** nothing in M7; runs beside the station work.
- **Done when:** an A/B sheet and short frame strips beside the GIF on the GPU, approved by the maintainer (H); the battle GPU test
  from M6 still inside its budget; tests green; committed.

- **Done (2026-11-13):**
  - **The maintainer's decisions** over four rounds of sheets: (1) **no pause** before a ship's own point flares (M6-T19 round 2's
    judgement stands; `point.lead_s` 0, a value there would hold the small star that long); (2) **round flashes with lens flares,
    no star rays** ("rather than having star rays from the flash, let's have lens flares for a more circular flash"); (3) **no
    white-out** at the gate's flash ("keep the flash but it doesn't need to white out the whole screen": a round flash 3.2 mouth
    radii across; the glow shader's `bloom` stays for data at 0); (4) the gate's slower opening from the GIF kept (open at 4.6 s,
    3.5 s before); (5) the dark eye stays 0.24 of the mouth (ships come out of that ring, M6-T22) and the lamps chase front to
    back along the rails (the GIF's gate has spoke arms); (6) the player's jump screen fade (`jumpgate_host.gd`, M6) untouched.
  - **The GIF's sequence with timings**: `docs/design-references.md`, "The GIF's sequence (M7-T23)" (the existing jumpgate doc):
    a table of each stage, its seconds in the GIF and the data key that carries it, and the amber hyperspace side recorded for M11.
  - **Data**: `data/jump/vortex_look.json` (`VortexLook`): `timing` (flash 1.4 s, unfolding 1.2 s, close 1 s, closing flash 1 s,
    hold 4 s), `gate` (lead-in 2.0 s, the lamps' chase 1.2 s, the arm ends throbbing twice, glow sizes, the flash's peak at 0.15,
    size, halo, ring, flare level and pink-red colour), `point` (no lead-in, the star's level, the flare's peak at 0.7, its
    blue-purple colour, `ring_start`, `eye_share`, `shallow`), `colours` (both directions and their brightness), `flare` (the size
    on screen a flash needs for a lens flare) and `look` (the funnel shader's uniforms). `VortexTiming`'s and `GateActivation`'s
    seconds are static values read from it (two `const` that used them, in `VortexTransit` and `BenchRunner`, became static).
  - **The gate**: the lamps chase in 1.2 s (0.6 s before), the arm ends glow pink-red and throb twice (520 m glows, 260 m before;
    lamps 120 m, 70 m before), the round flash bursts at once and falls fast to a pink-red spark, and the funnel grows from it as
    a blue disc with a small eye.
  - **A ship's own point** (`JumpVortex.own_point`): a blue-purple star swells to its flare (peak 0.7 into the flash), then the
    funnel springs out as a blue ring a third of the mouth across, wide and shallow (depth 0.35), and grows and deepens to the
    full funnel. Open 2.6 s after it is asked for (2.5 s before).
  - **The funnel**: the pale patches are now **feathers** (short tongues along the spiral in two sizes, with a slow sideways
    wander), in the patch layer's place in `VortexLod`, so the distance levels are M6's; the arrival's brightness 0.5 to 0.7, the
    hair streaks toned down; while it unfolds the look is laid out over what has unfolded, so a small disc or first ring is blue.
  - **Round flashes and the lens flare**: `jump_glow.gdshaderinc` draws a core, a soft halo and a thin lens ring; `rays` is 0 for
    the centre flashes, the lamps, the arm ends and the ships' ring flashes. The add-on's lens flare takes one light (the star), and
    the add-on is not edited: `PostEffects` appends a **second pass of the same effect** (a copy of the star's
    `SceneLensFlareEffect`) the first time a flash needs it and disables it when none does; `FlashFlare` (new, pure) takes each
    vortex's offer a frame (flare level x flash level x size on screen: none under 40 px of mouth radius, whole from 160) and
    gives the brightest the flare in its colour. One pass at most however many points flash. With the flash dead centre the
    ghosts sit on the light; off centre they separate (`M7-T23_lens_flare_off_centre.png`). The arm ends do not drive it, and a
    hull or the grown funnel in front hides it, as with the star's. Console: `post.flash_flare`.
  - **The battle GPU budget** (M6-T20's fps test, `bench battle 1000 10 seed=1`, 1920x1080, RX 6950 XT, Forward+, one run each,
    M6 then now, with the lens-flare pass; taken with the white-out variant, which the final values only make cheaper): points
    forming GPU 0.93 (p95 1.18) to 0.87 (1.27) ms, draw calls 181 to 171, fps 59.4 to 59.6; points open 1.03 (1.17) to 1.03
    (1.22) ms, 182 to 187, 59.8 to 60.0; the window 0.77 (0.89) to 0.76 (0.83) ms, 156 to 158; VRAM 571 MB both, 46 points open
    at most in both. Inside the budget. One gate in the close view: 4 draw calls at 60 fps.
  - **Tests**: new `test_vortex_look` (5: the file and its keys, both directions' colours, the code reading the file, every `look`
    key a shader uniform, a point and a gate opening their own ways with the maintainer's decisions) and `test_flash_flare` (4);
    `test_vortex_timing` rewritten to read the data's seconds (10); `test_battle_arrivals`, `test_jump_point_pool` and
    `test_vortex_transits` take a point's forming time from `VortexTiming` instead of 2.5 s. No GPU budget GUT test was added:
    the budget is M6's bench run above.
  - **Verified** on HEAD plus this task's files only (a `--staged` copy): import clean, the compile check (765 scripts), GUT 193
    passing and 3 pending headless in 23 files (the vortex, gate and jump files, `test_mission_runner`, `test_encounters`,
    `test_bench`, the far props, `test_post_effects`, `test_environment`), the main-scene smoke and a console run opening the
    gate's vortex and a point. No generator changed, so no Python test ran.
  - **Sheets** (H, approved as a direction over the rounds): `references/screenshots/M7-T23 jumpgate/`: `M7-T23_AB_gate_arrival.png`,
    `M7-T23_AB_jump_point.png` (the GIF, M6, now), `M7-T23_strip_gate_arrival.png`, `M7-T23_strip_jump_point.png`,

  **flat / lacking detail** (large plain areas), **colour and material wrong** against the references, and a **repeating, tiled
  look** (the 16 m detail tile or the panel pattern).

- **From the flicker fix (2026-11-14):** the frame's `radiators`, `spine`, `truss`, `vanes` and `bow` still overlap themselves in
  one plane and one paint (about 108 000 m², `HULL_SELF_OVERLAPS` in `test_coplanar_overlaps.py`); they can shimmer up close.
  Take them apart here and shrink that list.
- **What:**
  - Give the station the **two texture-detail sets** the ships have (M5-T40, M7-T15): `low` reads cleanly fullscreen on its own
    (major seams, plate tones, no blur at the views a player sees), `high` adds the fine plating, joint grime, recessed hatches and
    ribs; the setting switches them like the ships'.

    near-only detail props for fine marks, and mipmapping and filtering checked; VRAM measured and kept inside a budget the agent
    states (today 109 MB of maps, +14 MB props).
  - Detail: plating and panel layouts read off the references (T01's "Surface and panels", the station close-ups in
    `references/T40 seam references/`, the lighting and bay references), so large areas are never plain.
  - Colour and material: re-read the colours off lit captures as M5-T39 did, and re-check the metal and roughness against
    "metallic, not plastic" (D72); show swatches beside the captures.
  - No visible tiling: break up the 16 m detail tile (rotation and offset per plate, a second scale, masks), checked at grazing
    angles.
- **Starting point (2026-11-14):** T25 and T32 replaced the shared detail tile with the Earth Alliance mosaic (plates 0.8 to 3.2 m,
  varied both ways, `core/detail_layer.py`) station-wide and lined both bays in red-lit grey metal; the maintainer approved both.

### M7-T25 The main docking bay rebuilt to the STL and the show

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-14, watching the tour):** the main docking bay "doesn't look close enough to the show and needs
  revision"; all four faults named: **overall shape and proportions**, **the mouth and frame detail**, **the tunnel interior**,
  **colour and lighting**. T05 built it from the six close-up show captures (`references/docking bay references/`) and exempted
  it from the STL section check (scores 0.00 to 0.79 against 0.90 to 0.99 for every other part); the maintainer chose to take
  the **shape from the STL** and the **look from the show**.
- **Size (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** the main docking bay's entrance is **about 80 m wide**. The STL's measure and this estimate
  should agree; if they differ by more than about 10 %, the agent reports both before building. **First step:** measure the STL's
  main docking bay entrance at the 8 km scale (3.3743 m per unit), compare it with the 80 m estimate, and report the ratio and what
  it means for the cargo bay (built from the same STL: an octagonal opening about 68 x 31 m) before building anything. The passage for traffic (T05
  built 94 x 34 m; T10's civilians that fit it fly in) follows the new size, and T10 is told the new limits.
- **Agent:** Opus. **Depends on:** the flicker and light-leak fix committed (it seals the bays and keeps the sun out of
  interiors; the rebuild keeps both).
- **What:** read the main docking bay off `references/EA Babylon 5 Station.stl` with T21's section tools (the cross housing, the
  mouth and its frame, the tunnel's depth and steps, the airlock, the lower hatch, the toothed rim) and rebuild it to **IoU >= 0.90
  per section plane** (the exemption in `station_fidelity.py` removed); the look (the stepped red frames, dark machinery, red on
  edges and lamps, the sawtooth airlock, the pink panels at the mouth) from the six show captures and the bow-lights capture;
  the passage stays open for the traffic that fits (T10), the drum's roll kept, the bay sealed (no line of sight to space but the
  mouth), the sun kept out. Hardpoints (`main_docking_bay_*`), collision, sections and lights follow. A sheet: STL sections beside
  the build, and the bay from the bow, three-quarter and inside, beside the show captures (H); the maintainer may send more
  captures of the bay from outside.
- **Done when:** the sections pass, the sheet is approved, tests green, committed.
- **Phase 1, the STL measured (2026-11-14; the maintainer decides before the build):** the bay is in the STL, on the bow face, at
  the 8 km scale (3.3743 m a unit):
  - **Mouth:** a stepped cut in the frame block, **45 m wide and 20 m high** (a band 45 x 10 m across a core 27 x 20 m); clear
    inside its liner 40 x 16 m. **40.5 m deep**, from the block's face (z 480.4) to a back wall at z 520.9 carrying the closed
    sawtooth doors (the show's inner airlock). Nothing behind the doors: the STL is solid there.
  - **Frame block:** an octagon 95 x 51 m (corners cut to a flat top 62 m wide), face z 480.4, slatted grilles over and under the
    mouth, marked panels at its corners; a fan-shaped grilled housing each side (x 35 to 58) stands forward to z 449.
  - **Cross housing:** the bar across 226 m (x +-113, y +-13.5, face z 480.4); the bar up and down 40 m wide at the block, 26 m at
    its ends, its face sloping back from z 490 to 496. **Toothed ring** r 107 to 139, front z 470 to 475. The disc behind is recessed:
    r 59.5 to 107 at z 505 to 513, r 43 to 59.5 to z 517.
  - **Against 80 m:** the mouth is 0.56 of it (80 m would be 1.78 times the STL's), the frame block 1.19; neither within 10 %.
    Today's build: block 140 x 112 m with its face at z 450, mouth frame 116 m, passage 94 x 34, tunnel to z 640; section IoU 0.11
    to 0.65 at z 476 to 519.
  - **The cargo bay:** already the STL's (T21: throat 68.2 x 31.4 m, compartment x +-94). The same scale reading leaves it as it is.
    Scaling it by 1.78 to match an 80 m mouth would make the compartment 335 m wide, wider than the spine (x +-166), so the STL's
    bays read as consistent with the 8 km hull.
  - **The check:** the STL's bow is overlapping shells with mixed winding, so the nonzero fill calls the mouth solid at every z
    plane; the docking bay's planes compare the face as seen from ahead (a relief section: solid where the first surface from the
    bow lies before the plane).
  - **Traffic:** at the STL's size none of T10's tunnel hulls fit (Skylark 40 x 10, Narn transport 46 x 18, Brezebel 39 x 25).
  - Sheet: `references/screenshots/M7-T25 docking bay/M7-T25_phase1_sheet.png` (gitignored).
- **Maintainer's choice (2026-11-14), signed exception to the 90 % rule:** an **80 m mouth**: the frame block and the mouth (its
  depth, grilles, panels, stepped cut, sawtooth doors) scaled by **80/45 (1.778)** about the axis and the block's face, the toothed
  ring and the cross housing at the STL's size; the section check runs against the STL **locally scaled** the same way. Why: T10's
  inbound civilians must fit (the STL's 45 m mouth takes none of them).
- **Added (maintainer, 2026-11-14, watching T10's docking):** "the interior doors don't open and the ship does not disappear into
  the open doors. Expecting a black opening behind the interior doors that the docking ship moves into, and then when fully behind
  the black veil, the doors close." The sawtooth doors open and close (data-driven timing), a black void behind them; a docking
  ship flies through, the doors close once it is wholly behind their plane and it leaves the world; undocking the reverse.

  - **Traffic** (`StationTraffic`, `DockingPlan`): a docking ship asks the doors open with its nose 400 m out, flies the inner leg

    metal (a plating cell in the prop atlas, the piece's detail tile over them: `PropKind.world_detail`, `PieceSections.prop_detail`,
    `MeshAssets.prop_mesh`), the seam no longer self-lit, the doorway's lip grey; the void stays black. The shared detail tile
    (`core/detail_layer.py`, the whole station and the hangar) is now the Earth Alliance mosaic in both directions. Draw calls
    unchanged, triangles +7 k, Godot VRAM +0.2 MB, GPU about +0.1 to +0.9 ms in the tunnel. Tests: `test_station_hardpoints`
    (no self-lit rings, only lamps; the plating; the doors' metal and detail tile), `test_babylon5_station` (the mosaic varies both
    ways), GUT `test_bay_lights`. Sheet: `references/screenshots/T25 red-lit tunnel/T25_red_lit_tunnel_sheet.png`
    (`SHEET_VIEWS=t25tunnel`).
  - **The pink band-end panels were removed** with the red strips and the lit frames (the maintainer: no glowing rings or bands;
    they stay removed).
  - **The mosaic replaced the shared plating tile station-wide**: `core/detail_layer.py` is laid over the whole station's hull and
    over the hangar behind the menu (`b5_interior`), not only the bay (the maintainer: it stays station-wide).
  - **Cost** (RX 6950 XT, Forward+, X11 fullscreen 6 144 x 3 456; triangles +7 k, Godot's VRAM 1 891.7 to 1 891.9 MB):
