### M7-T26 All ships keep a low-polygon model at range

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-14, flying the station showcase):** "when I was more than about 2.7 km away from the Saturn 3, it
  turned into a grey sphere, even though it was still visible. I was expecting a lower polygon model, not a sphere." Then: "update T26 to be all ships. Even the
  Narn transport had the grey sphere issue." **Every ship**, whatever its size, keeps its shape (a low-polygon level) until it is
  only a few pixels across.
- **What:** find which far representation draws the grey sphere (M4's far hulls or dots, M6's far props, the ship LOD chain) and
  why it switches by distance rather than by size on screen. Ships keep their real silhouette at range: a generated low-polygon LOD
  (or the existing chain's coarsest level) until the ship is a few pixels across, then the far dot; the switch by screen size, so a
  100 m Saturn holds its shape far longer than a 10 m fighter. Applies to every hull (civilians, capitals' stand-ins, fighters).
  Within the battle budget (M4's 1 000-ship bench, M6's battle test).
- **Agent:** Opus. **Done when:** a strip of a Saturn 3 and a Starfury from 500 m to 50 km showing the shape held, then a dot; the
  bench inside budget; tests (switch by screen size); the maintainer has seen it (H).
- **Done (2026-11-14, accepted live by the maintainer flying the station showcase: "t26 appears to be correct and approved"):**
  - **The grey sphere:** M4's far dot. `LodSwitcher` turned every AI ship into a `FarDots` point at a fixed 3 km
    (`far_ship_dot_m`), and the dot is drawn at the hull's true angular size: a 70 m Saturn 3 at 3 km is a soft grey disc 35 px
    across on 1 080 lines, a Narn transport 20 px. Tier rows (beyond 10 km) other than capitals were dots too.
  - **Approach:** the importer's levels of detail (already on every hull, `generate_lods`), not a generated chain: the renderer
    picks them by size on screen with under a pixel of error, so the shape holds by construction, and they cost no asset, no
    VRAM and no node (M4-T30d measured a generated far level had nothing to win). What changed is when the mesh gives way to
    the dot: by size on screen, `settings.far_ship_dot_px` (2 px of bounding radius, about 3 px across the hull; replaces
    `far_ship_dot_m`), with M4's tenth of hysteresis. `LodSwitcherMath.dot_distance_m`, `LodSwitcher.dot_scale` (the view's
    pixels per radian, `FarProp.k_of`).
  - **Tier rows:** a demoted ship's row now carries its hull's far class (`FarHulls`) and its paint; the core decides each frame
    which rows are a few pixels across (`tier_set_view`, a visual-only `dotted` column with the same hysteresis) and draws those
    as dots, the rest as hulls. Each far-hull class's bounds follow its instances, so the importer's levels work there too (a
    world-sized box held every far hull at full detail). Capitals follow the same rule (a carrier never gets that small).
  - **Switch distances** at 1 080 lines and 75 degrees (farther on a taller window; 2400x1350 on the maintainer's Wayland
    desktop is 1.25 times these):

    | hull | radius m | dot past | triangles, full to coarsest level |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Starfury | 9.9 | 3.5 km | 34 352, 17 176, 8 588, 4 294, 1 650 |
    | raider | 8.8 | 3.1 km | 10 540, 7 086 |
    | Saturn 3 | 70 | 25 km | 41 902, 20 950, 10 474, 5 088, 834 |
    | Saturn 4 transport / tanker | 96 / 85 | 34 / 30 km | 45 586 to 224 / 44 082 to 332 |
    | Saturn 8 transport / tanker | 140 / 133 | 49 / 47 km | 55 382 to 192 / 52 890 to 98 |
    | Narn transport | 43 | 15 km | 5 692, 2 846, 990 |
    | Brezebel / Bok'Mai / Skylark | 38 / 46 / 37 | 13 / 16 / 13 km | 16 810 to 314 / 9 284 to 226 / 26 052 to 1 000 |

    Budget per hull: at most 8 000 triangles at the coarsest level, under 70 % of the full mesh (tested). The raider is the
    weak one (7 086, 67 %): its thin open panels leave the importer little to fold; it was drawn that way to 3 km before, so
    nothing got worse, and a generated far level for it is a follow-up if a bench ever shows it. The freighters' riding pods
    follow their ship's dot (`FreighterCargo`); the 6 m cargo pod craft (M7-T33, T34) is not a ship and keeps its own 20 km
    draw range.
  - **GPU** (RX 6950 XT, RADV, Forward+, Wayland, window 2400x1350), before and after: the station's bow with traffic (two
    freighters, two dockings, an undocking, eight raiders): 280 to 290 draw calls and 281 to 291, 512k to 532k triangles and
    521k to 542k, 145 fps (the display's cap) and 1.0 to 1.4 ms GPU both. `bench battle 1000 10 seed=1` (measured window):
    draw calls median 95 and 127, triangles 485k and 606k, GPU median 1.20 and 1.22 ms (p95 1.54 and 1.58), Godot's VRAM
    787 MB both, sysfs `card1` peak 4.67 GB both. **Budget:** at `battle 1000` GPU median under 2 ms and p95 under 2.5 ms,
    draw calls under 200, no VRAM growth: held.
  - **Strip (H, approved):** `references/screenshots/M7-T26 ships at range/M7-T26_ships_at_range_strip.png`: the Saturn 3, the
    Starfury and the Narn transport from 0.5 to 49 km, before and after, each tile cropped round the ship and enlarged. Before,
    the Saturn and the Narn are grey blobs from 3.5 km; after, both hold their shape to 10 and 20 km, then a dot.
  - **Tests:** `test_ship_lod_at_range` (new: every hull's levels and its budget, the same levels on every load, every AI hull
    from 500 m to 50 km and back a mesh until under the dot size and never a dot bigger than it, a Saturn 3 and a Narn
    transport meshes at 5 km where a Starfury is a dot, the switch following the window and the field of view);
    `test_lod_switcher_math` (the dot distance); `test_far_dots`, `test_frame_passes`, `test_textured_hull` on the pixel rule;
    `test_tier_proxies` (a tier freighter's hull at 15 km, a dot at 45 km, never both; the classes' bounds); Rust
    `tier::tests` (3 new: by size not distance, the hysteresis and a repeated view, no hull class or no view).
  - `sheet` parks a ship up to 50 km out (was 20 km) for the strips.
  - **Fix (CI on 5886fd7d):** every demoted ship now makes a far-hull class, and those multimeshes sat in World space without
    being `world_shiftable` (`test_rebase_in_play`): each class is shiftable now and on `rebased` goes back to the origin with its
    instances and bounds moved instead, as `FarDots` does. And headless test windows are 64 px tall, so the pixel rule made a
    raider a dot 180 m out and hid its nozzle lights and markers (`test_ship_lights`, `test_running_lights_in_play`): the rule
    never counts fewer than 1 080 lines (`LodSwitcherMath.MIN_VIEW_LINES`, `view_k`), so a small window or a 960x540 capture
    keeps ships as meshes as far as a 1 080-line screen would. Tests: `test_tier_proxies` (the rebase), `test_ship_lod_at_range`
    (a small window counts as 1 080 lines; a 2 160-line window doubles the distance), `test_lod_switcher_math` (`view_k`).
  - **Fix (a timing-dependent far-prop failure, full GUT run on 065ac150):** `test_the_station_shrinks_both_pieces_and_its_drum_keeps_turning`
    measured the station 500 km off after the camera was cut to 1 500 km. `FarProp` read the camera's interpolated pose, and for a
    node with interpolation off Godot can return the last frame's place, depending on whether a frame ran in between (reproduced
    1 run in 4 after `test_every_world_core`, without load). `FarProp.drawn_pose` reads a node that is not interpolated at its
    pose now (`ScaledSpace` too); the test's `reset_physics_interpolation` (75993ca2) did nothing and is gone. Test: the pair 8
    runs green; `test_a_camera_cut_this_frame_is_read_where_it_is_now`. The sandbox rebase's cost is unchanged by the far hulls'
    rebase handler (median 377 to 411 us before T26, 315 to 394 after, 332 to 430 with the handler off; the 525 us was the 3x
    parallel run's load); the 500 us budget stays.

### M7-T27 Navigation targets and the hold-X interaction

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** "Babylon 5 docking bay, Babylon 5 cargo bay, and jumpgate must be navigation targets (not
  civilian targets)"; "when the docking bay, cargo bay, or jumpgate are targeted, you must hold X for 2 seconds to initiate the
  appropriate action for that element (only valid for jumpgate currently, but eventually will be able to enter the docking bay
  and/or offload/load cargo, depending on the mission). Show a progress bar across the top of the screen to show it's working.
  This will allow the normal X press to change auto-target. The goal is to create a new long hold X mechanic for interactions
  with different objects."
- **What:** the main docking bay, the cargo bay and the jumpgate are **navigation targets** (their own target class and HUD look,
  not civilian ships; the station itself stays as it is). A general **interaction** seam: a target can offer an action; with it
  targeted, **holding X for 2 s** runs it, with a progress bar across the top of the screen that fills over the hold and cancels on
  release; a **tap** of X keeps toggling auto-track as today. In M7 only the jumpgate's action works (the player's gate jump, M6);
  the docking bay and cargo bay offer a placeholder ("not available") that missions later fill (docking, loading, unloading).
  Pad and keyboard both (the keyboard's auto-track key held the same way); the hold time and the bar are data and settings.
- **Done when:** tests (tap toggles auto-track, hold 2 s runs the action, release cancels, the bar's progress, the three targets are
  navigation targets), a live run for the maintainer (H), committed.
- **Done (as built; contracts in `docs/architecture.md` §20.5; accepted live by the maintainer, 2026-11-15):**
  - **Navigation targets.** `Targetable.Role.BEACON` is every navigation target: the nav buoys, the jumpgate (it was a neutral
    ship-like target, so it fell under CIVILIAN) and the station's bays, `NavPoint`s at the main docking bay's mouth and the cargo
    bay's entry (`BABYLON 5 DOCKING BAY`, `BABYLON 5 CARGO BAY`; neutral props, heard as far as the station). The station itself is
    unchanged. The HUD draws a navigation target as a cyan diamond (the target menu's NAVIGATION colour) with no lead; ships keep
    their brackets. A bay is a place, not a thing: the core knows it as a navigation marker (native `ROLE_NAV`) and avoidance
    flies through it (found by `test_station_traffic`: a docking Skylark dodged the marker on its lane into the tunnel wall).
  - **The interaction seam.** A `Targetable` may carry a `TargetInteraction` (kind, label, offer, action). The bays carry `dock`
    and `cargo` placeholders that answer "not available"; the gate's is the player's jump (`JumpgateHost.start_jump`), offered as
    before. `InteractionHost` (Main's child) offers the selected target's interaction, runs it at the end of the hold and shows
    `HOLD X: <label>` or the answer (`BABYLON 5 DOCKING BAY: NOT AVAILABLE`, 3 s) on the acknowledgement line.
  - **Tap and hold.** `HoldInteract`: with nothing offered X toggles auto-track on the press, as before. With an interaction
    offered, a release within 0.35 s is the tap (auto-track), a 2 s hold fires the interaction once, and a release in between
    cancels it. The tap threshold is the target button's (`settings.target_hold_s`), so both tap-or-hold buttons split at the same
    time and one setting tunes both; the bar shows only after it, so a tap never flashes it. The hold time is
    `settings.interact_hold_s` (2.0, 0.5 to 5). The bar runs across the top of the window (60 % wide, above the status line), in
    the chase and cockpit views. Keyboard (Shift, Mouse 4) and pad (X) alike, through `InputAdapter`.
  - **Console:** provider and command `interact` (`status`, `run`, `hold [s]`), in `info.md`.
  - **Tests:** `test_hold_interact` (8), `test_nav_interaction` (11), `test_nav_targets` (3: the three are navigation targets and
    not civilian, a bay's 2 s hold answers not available, a tap still tracks, the console), `test_gate_jump` and
    `test_jumpgate_in_play` (the jump now takes a 2 s hold), `test_hud` and `test_target_menu` (two more broadcasters, the buoy's
    navigation look), Rust `avoidance_flies_through_a_navigation_marker`.
  - **Capture:** `references/screenshots/M7-T27 hold-X/` (each bay's and the gate's marker, the bar mid-hold in the chase and
    cockpit views, the bay's answer).
  - **Fix round (maintainer's flight, 2026-11-14: "wouldn't allow the target to change from the docking bay to the cargo bay or
    to the jumpgate").** Two causes, both in the target menu (B held). Its NAVIGATION ring made every pick the waypoint, never
    the target (M5-T25's rule for beacons), so picking the cargo bay or the gate moved the waypoint and left the docking bay
    selected; and the ring holds the nearest five, so beside the station the buoys pushed the gate (50 km out) off it. Now a
    navigation target that offers an interaction is a target pick and leads the ring (buoys are still waypoints). The console,
    the wheel and target ahead already worked; the target button's tap is the nearest enemy by design (now tested from a bay);
    auto-track picks only when nothing is selected. The gate's hold is offered whenever it is targeted; beyond 2 km the gate's
    refusal (M7-T28) shows as `JUMPGATE: OUT OF RANGE`. Tests: `test_nav_targets` (+4: the menu, the console, the wheel and
    target ahead each move the selection between the bays and the gate; the tap takes an enemy from a bay; the gate's hold at
    3 km and at 1.8 km), `test_target_menu` and `test_target_categories` (+1 each).
  - **Keyboard parity (maintainer's flight on keyboard and mouse: "no keyboard equivalent of holding X").** The audit of the input
    map, the input source and the menus found no pad-only action: the hold is the `autotrack` action, so Shift and Mouse 4 already
    tapped and held as pad X does, but the HUD said `HOLD X`, and the keyboard's X is the brake. The prompt now names the live
    bindings (`HOLD X / SHIFT / MOUSE 4: DOCK`, `BindingNames`). `test_keyboard_parity`: every action on the pad has a key or
    mouse button in each built-in layout (pitch and yaw are the mouse's motion), the radial menus have keys, and a real Shift
    through `GodotInputSource` taps (auto-track), holds 2 s (the interaction, once, the bar full) and cancels when let go early.
  - **Reach (maintainer: "don't prompt to press X unless the player is within range").** Every interaction has a reach (the gate
    its 2 km request range, each bay 2 km). Out of reach: no button prompt, no bar, no progress, X is auto-track alone; the line
    reads `JUMPGATE: 3.4 km — CLOSE TO 2.0 km TO JUMP`. Within reach: `HOLD X / SHIFT / MOUSE 4: JUMP` (the gate's label is now
    JUMP). `test_nav_targets`: the gate at 3 km and 1.8 km, a bay at 5 km and 1.5 km (status, no bar, nothing runs; then the
    buttons, the bar, one run); `test_nav_interaction`: reach and status text.
  - **Fix (found by CI, 2026-11-15):** with the places leading the NAVIGATION ring, `test_target_preview`'s buoy check
    highlighted a bay (the ring's first slice). The test now picks the buoy by name, and the preview names a bay (`Docking bay`,
    `Cargo bay`, no hull of its own) and the gate (`Jumpgate`, its own wireframe) instead of a bare faction; new checks in
    `test_target_preview` and `test_target_preview_model`. No other test reads the ring's order.

### M7-T28 Fix: the jumpgate's second entry

- **Accepted (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** flown live: one vortex on a second entry, no roll, the camera tracking, no target square in the cinematic, the 2 km rule.

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** "when entering the jumpgate a second time, the jumpgate's vortex activated twice; the second
  time the camera didn't track the ship correctly."
- **What:** reproduce (jump through the gate, return, jump again), find the state that isn't reset (gate activation, vortex
  instances, the camera's follow target or the jump sequence), fix at the source, test two and three jumps in a row.
- **Done:**
  - Reproduced on the main scene (free flight, three jumps in a row, logged per tick). A second jump asked 3 s after the first
    opened the orange vortex twice (openings 3 and 4), and the departure shot's camera swung up to 130 degrees off the gate
    (173 degrees when asked at once, where the vortex opened once but stood open 15 s while the ship dawdled).
  - **Root cause:** no stale state. `GateJump` ended the line-up on position alone (within 200 m of the departure point). A ship
    just back from a jump is between the gate and that point flying away at 200 m/s, so it crossed the point outbound and the
    run-in began with the ship leaving. The gate opened the vortex (ship within 1 600 m), the ship overshot out of range while it
    turned round, the vortex timed out and closed, and opened again when the ship came back. The departure shot rides the ship's
    own frame, so it swung round with the ship as it flipped. The first jump never showed it: it reaches the point inbound.
  - **Fix:** the line-up ends only when the ship is at the departure point and not flying away from the gate
    (`GateJump.LINE_UP_RECEDE_MAX_MPS`, 10 m/s; `JumpgateHost` feeds the speed toward the gate along the departure axis). The
    turn-round now happens under the chase camera, before the vortex and the shot start.
  - **Tests:** `test_gate_jump` (the run-in waits for an outbound ship, starts for one inbound or at rest);
    `test_jumpgate_in_play` flies three jumps in a row (the second asked 3 s after the first, the third at once): one orange and
    one blue opening each, the cinematic camera on the player's own ship every tick, within 200 m, the ship in the picture, and
    the camera within 35 degrees of the gate's axis all the way in (15 to 23 degrees now; 130 and 173 before).
  - **Watch it:** `.tools/godot/godot --path . -- --console-exec="wait 120; gate approach; gate jump; wait 3500; gate jump"`
    (the second jump starts about 3 s after the first hands the controls back; `state gate` then reads 4 openings, 2 completed).
- **Three more from the maintainer's flight (2026-11-14):**
  1. **A green square showed where the jump point was about to form.** It was the debug readout's target square
     (`TargetBracket`, M1-T15), which `Main` draws on the selected target whenever the HUD is hidden. A cinematic hides the HUD,
     so during the jump's shots the square sat on the targeted gate's centre. Fixed: no square while a cinematic plays. GPU
     frames before and after in `references/screenshots/M7-T28 gate/`.
  2. **Re-entering at once, the Starfury sometimes rolled.** The run-in (and the flight down the funnel) rolled every ship level
     with the gate's up. The first jump arrives level, so nothing showed; a ship that had just turned round came out of the turn
     up to 150 degrees off and was rolled back at 90 degrees a second (measured: 89, 149, 49 and 94 degrees of roll from four
     starting rolls). Fixed: a ship that fits the mouth any way up keeps its roll (`GateAutopilot.roll_up`); only one wider than
     half the mouth's half height is still turned, as before. Measured after: under 10 degrees in all, never above 5 degrees a
     second.
  3. **A jump may be asked only within 2 km of the gate.** `JumpgateHost.jump_refusal(gate) -> String` ("" or why not) and
     `can_request_jump(gate) -> bool` are the rule; `start_jump` and the console's `gate jump` go through it. Beyond the range
     the answer is `out of range` (the HUD shows `JUMPGATE: OUT OF RANGE`). The range and the answer are data:
     `data/jump/gate_request.json`, read by `GateJumpRequest`. `gate approach` takes a distance in km (`gate approach 1.9`).
     The prompt's 10 km (`GateJumpPrompt.RANGE_M`) is unchanged here; T27 owns what the HUD offers.
  - **Tests:** `test_gate_jump_request` (the range and answer from data, `roll_up`, the run-in commands no roll);
    `test_jumpgate_in_play`: a request 2.3 km out is refused and nothing starts, 1.8 km out it starts; three starting rolls just
    out of the gate reach the ring with under 10 degrees of roll (86 to 94 before); no square in any tick of the three jumps'
    shots (about 1 000 ticks each before).
  - **Watch it:** `.tools/godot/godot --path . -- --console-exec="wait 120; gate approach; gate jump; gate approach 1.9; gate jump; wait 2250; gate jump"`
    (refused at 6 km, a jump from 1.9 km, then again about 3 s after the controls come back).

### M7-T29 Fix: civilians attacked in free flight don't flee

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** "when attacking a civilian vessel in free flight mode, it failed to run. No change in velocity or
  direction."
- **What:** reproduce in free flight; find whether the civilian's brain never hears the attack (perception, the attacker memory,
  the faction relation), the flee behaviour is suppressed (M7-T10's new station rules, the idle brain T11 used for the parked
  Saturn), or the civilians spawned in free flight lack a brain; fix; a test that an attacked civilian changes heading and speed
  and flees toward its own faction (M5's rule).
- **Decisions (maintainer, 2026-11-14):**
  1. **Fear, no damage.** The player's bolts still pass through a friendly or neutral civilian (D31 stands). A civilian flees
     from any ship whose fire comes close, toward its own faction, and raises the alarm for its kin. Gated on the civilian
     role, so no parity recording moves.
  2. **Turrets:** "if the civilian was targeted first, then they fire back. if it was stray fire, then they stay silent." A
     shooter that had that civilian selected when its close fire came may be engaged by that civilian's turrets, and their
     bolts land on that shooter only; fire from a ship with something else selected is fled from in silence.
- **Root cause.** Not a T10 regression and not a missing brain: the civilian never heard the attack. Every gate was keyed on
  faction hostility. The player is `earth_alliance`; the Saturns and the Skylark are too, and the Narn, Centauri and Brakiri
  hulls are neutral to it. So the player's bolts passed through (`Damageable.can_be_hit_by`: no hit, no attacker), were not
  counted as incoming fire (`contact::incoming_one`: hostile bolts only), and the retreat scores 0 with no hostile in sight.
  Reproduced on the build before the fix with the new test: the fighter fired, the Saturn stayed idle and moved 0 m.
- **Done (as built; contracts in `docs/architecture.md` §18.11; awaiting the maintainer's check in free flight):**
  - **The scare (native, in the AI job).** A civilian's think looks for a bolt of any side, not its own, that passes within
    its hull's radius plus 15 m in the incoming-fire horizon (`contact::close_fire_from`, through the bolts' grid; the
    earliest bolt row decides). When its shooter is among the contacts and not hostile (a hostile's fire is M5's rule,
    unchanged), the retreat keeps that body for 20 s (`RetreatState.scare`, the panic's hold): it scores as a threat in
    sight does, the flight is M5's (`civilian::flee_from`, to kin, a warship's far side, closing up with fleeing kin) with
    the shooter never taken as the kin to run to (`View.shun`), and the civilian counts as alarmed, so kin within 5 km run
    too (Q25).
  - **Meant or stray.** When the shooter's selection (`rows.target_ids`) is that civilian, the think names it
    (`Brain.provoked_by`); the job lists the pair (`PickJob.provoked`, in the batch's order) and the main thread notes it at
    the join in the turrets' memory (`Memory.note_provoked`, 30 s as any attacker, refreshed while the fire goes on). A
    provoked pair lets **that civilian's own turrets** pick the shooter though it is a friend (`turret_kernels::pick`; a
    neighbour's turrets stay silent), and lets **that civilian's bolts land on that shooter** though their sides are not
    hostile (`HitResolver` asks `ScsSimCore.provoked_pair` only while `provoked_count` is above 0). Nothing else changes
    who can be hit: the shooter's bolts still pass through the civilian.
  - **Measured** (`test_civilian_attacked`, a Saturn 4 with one turret, a Starfury of its own people 700 m astern holding
    its trigger 4 s): stray fire: the Saturn went from rest to 59 m/s and 588 m toward its kin in 20 s, its kin 3 km off
    ran too, 0 turret shots, no damage; with the Saturn selected, 12 s of fire: 16 turret shots, 7 hits on the shooter, 0
    on a second Starfury beside it, 0 on the Saturn.
  - **Threads and parity.** Threaded against delayed and one thread against six: the same rows, commands and turret digests
    tick for tick with the provocation live. `turret_digest` adds a word only for a provoked pair, so every digest without
    one is as it was. No recording moved.
  - **Tests:** Rust 176 (+3: the scare and its end, meant against stray and a wide miss and a fighter left alone, the
    provoked turret and its neighbours). GUT `test_civilian_attacked` (3).
  - **Not changed:** T10's lane traffic needs nothing: a frightened lane ship runs for 20 s after the last close shot and
    then takes its lane up again.

### M7-T30 Maintenance bots carry the freighters' cargo pods

- **Accepted (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** watched live with T34's cargo pods doing the carrying (T34 replaces the bots as carriers; T30's plan, routes and timing are what the pods fly).

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** "maintenance pods should be grabbing and moving the Saturn 3's cargo pods. Same with a Saturn 4
  or 8, for either cargo or tankers." This replaces T10's pods flying into the cargo bay by themselves.
- **What:** when a freighter has backed in at the cargo bay's hold point (T10), maintenance bots fly out, grab each cargo pod (or tank
  on the tankers), carry it into the cargo bay and come back; the reverse for loading. Saturn 3, Saturn 4 (transport and tanker) and
  Saturn 8 (transport and tanker), each by its own pod layout from M5's modular Saturns. Bots and pods ambient as before; clearance
  tested; hide when fighting starts (pods wait). A live run for the maintainer (H).
- **Maintainer's choices (2026-11-14):** transport pods carried as whole blocks (no generator change); one bot a Saturn 3 pod, two
  (front and back) a transport block or a tank; tanks turned 90 degrees onto their side before the bay.
- **Done (as built, 2026-11-14; awaiting the maintainer's live run):**
  - **Cargo.** `FreighterCargo` (T10's `FreighterPods` renamed; the self-flying pods are gone): the chunks named `pod*`/`tank*` are
    cargo on all five Saturns (3, 2, 2, 4, 4); the hull hides while they are off, the other chunks ride the ship, a pod on the hull is
    the ship's child and a carried one is in the station's frame. Loading brings the Saturn in without its pods and puts the hull back
    when the last is on.
  - **Plan.** `CargoFerryPlan.for_freighter` from the chunks' boxes and the collision shapes: pods nearest the bay first; per pod a
    `CargoTrip` (slide clear of front mounts, lift outboard, aft past the stern, onto the bay's middle, in until wholly behind the
    back wall; stops at the corners off the hull), each bot's hold 8 m off its end and its route out (behind the stern, in from the
    side). A bot whose end is boxed in on the hull takes hold after the lift (the Saturn 8's inner ends). Tanks turn onto their side
    120 m long, done 80 m before the leading face reaches the mouth. The Saturn 3's 1 m pylon gap is now 6 m (slide margin).
  - **Carrying.** `FerryPod` (moves only while every bot it needs holds it), `CargoFerryJob` (pod by pod, a crew of two
    `BotFerryMotion`s in the fleet), `BotFerryMotion` carrying (out, hold, carry, home; T11's shuttle unchanged for `bots cargo`).
    Fighting: away from the mouth the bots let go and go home (the far one over the pod), the pod waits, they come back after the
    quiet; near the mouth they carry on in (unloading) or back in (loading).
  - **Traffic.** `traffic freighter [type] [near|hold|show] [load]` (`show`: backing in its last 300 m, the bots already on their
    way, berth re-plans from where it stopped, the player beside the lane; first pod taken about 75 s after the command); the cycle picks any of the five (auto: hull and loading from the
    seed); the hold lasts until the bots are done (`DockingPlan.WAIT_S` 1800 s as a cap), then a lane from the hold out; `pods_in`,
    `pods_out`, `cargo` in the provider. `DockingPlan.pod_path` dropped.
  - **Fixes found on the way.** The plan uses the station's own frame (its frame piece is drawn closer while the station is far
    off); a pod creeping into a stop at the main scene's 1/480 s step slipped past its second bot's grab point (now snapped).
  - **Tests** (from a `--staged --build-native` copy): `test_cargo_ferry_carry` 6/6 (399 asserts), `test_ferry_pod` 8/8 (86),
    `test_cargo_carry_clearance` 5/5 (1545; least: bot 11.7 m hull, 7.3 m Saturn; pod 6.0 m Saturn, 5.0 m in the bay's mouth),
    `test_station_traffic` 4/4 (44), slow `test_freighter_cargo` 3/3 (all five hulls unload and leave, loading, the combat pause;
    about 6 min); neighbours unchanged: `test_bot_ferry_motion` 6/6, `test_bot_ferry_fleet` 4/4, `test_cargo_ferry_plan` 5/5,
    `test_cargo_ferry_clearance` 3/3, `test_docking_plan` 4/4, `test_bots_on_station` 8/8, `test_station_ai` 2/2,
    `test_station_tour` 8/8, `test_slow_tests` 3/3; compile check clean; main-scene smoke clean.
  - **GPU sheet** (Forward+, RX 6950 XT, about 145 fps, 265 draw calls, 684 MB VRAM): `references/screenshots/M7-T30 cargo pods/`,
    three frames a hull from `traffic freighter <type> show` (backing in at 20 s, the bots taking the first pod at 80 s, carried
    at 105 s). The look and the feel stay (H): the maintainer's live run, one line a hull (`show` puts the player beside the lane on
    the side whose pods come off first; the bots are small at that distance).

### M7-T31 The pause menu's Quit and compact menus

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** "in the pause menu, add a quit option that exits the game entirely"; "in the pause menu and start
  menu, shrink each of the boxes to fit only as much as is being displayed for the main views. Settings can use its current
  layout. But for everything else, it ends up being a lot of space taken up that just feels empty."
- **What:** Quit in the pause menu (with a confirm, pad-reachable), leaving the game; the start menu's and pause menu's main views
  (and their simple sub-pages, not Settings) sized to their content; M5's front-end tests and pad-only navigation unchanged; a sheet
  (H).
- **Done (2026-11-14, maintainer-approved from the sheet):**
  - **Quit.** The pause list ends with Quit to desktop. It opens a "Quit to desktop?" page (`ConfirmScreen`) with No first and focused, so a
    stray double A never quits; B and No step back, Yes leaves the game. Pad-reachable; `PauseMenu.quit_game` is injectable for tests.
  - **Compact boxes.** The start screen, New game, Free flight, the pause list and the confirm page size each box to its content
    (`MenuScreen.compact`, `MenuFrame` compact layout). Settings, Controls and the debrief keep the filled layout.
  - **Pause menu.** The pad box is docked to the lower left corner (48 px from the left, 40 px from the bottom) and shrinks in a small
    window instead of meeting the options box; the objectives box stays 440 px, top aligned right of the left column, narrowing in
    a narrow window.
  - **Descriptions.** New game's briefing and Free flight's note sit in a box on the right (`MenuScreen.wide_right`): top aligned beside
    the options box, from the 24 px gap to the right margin, as tall as the text, capped at the window and scrolling when long (no
    scroll indicator, by the maintainer's decision). It follows the focus and is hidden while Back has the focus.
  - **Tests.** `test_menu_compact` (box heights, the pad dock, the description box at 1920x1080, 1920x1200, 3840x2160, 1100x700 and
    800x600), `test_pause_quit` (confirm, cancel, Yes, pad only); `test_pause_menu` lists five entries and `test_front_end` expects the
    description box on New game and Free flight, the only M5 assertions that changed. Sheet tooling: `scripts/menu_sheet.sh`.

### M7-T14 Integration, full suite, docs and acceptance

- **Goal:** AC11 and the (H) checklist for AC12.
- **Steps:** §20 in `docs/architecture.md` as built; the M7 section of `docs/playtest.md` with the (H) checklist; the roadmap row;
  `scripts/run_tests.sh` once, read from the suite's own totals; the GPU check; then ask the maintainer to confirm the milestone,
  and once confirmed merge `m7` into `main`.
- **Done when:** the maintainer confirms M7.

- **Scope added (2026-11-15):**
  - The council's call-site tests: a heavy cannon in `TurretRows` (a lifted mount) hits a crossing fighter-sized target at 2 km,
    10-30 degrees up, and an interceptor battery's stored azimuth and elevation follow the pulse it fires; each fails when its line in
    `turret_rows.rs` is reverted.
  - `frame drop <km> [body]` (maintainer, 2026-11-15): a console command that puts the player at rest against a body (the planet or
    the star) at that altitude, not in orbit, so the ship visibly falls in (the HUD's ALT counting down; the atmosphere's impact as
    M6 built it); `info.md` and a test.
  - `tests/integration/test_gravity_orbit.gd`'s `test_a_ship_in_jolt_coasts_beside_the_station_as_the_64_bit_run_does` fails on the
    maintainer's 20-thread machine (Jolt's "job system exceeded the maximum number of jobs", from 90fa7f7a on; CI passes): find the
    cause and make it pass locally without hiding the error.
  - The GPU tests that read the middle of the window where GUT's panel sits (`test_sun_gpu.gd`'s disc height and colour,
    `test_scaled_space_gpu.gd`'s three; found by T44): sample clear of the panel or hide it.
  - `scripts/gut_shards.py`: the weights of the files T43 sped up (`test_cargo_carry_clearance` 600 s -> its measured time) and the new
    files' weights from the latest CI run.
  - The stale "Done (as built, 2026-11-14 ...)" cargo notes under this section: they describe M7-T30/T34's work; move them to the
    task they belong to.
  - The M4 to M6 hands-on checks: the maintainer ticks them on the checklist page (https://claude.ai/artifact/FhZSkHy4ZckNQkm22Kn7PA);
    the controller reads the ticks and notes back and updates `docs/playtest.md`.
- **Done (code part, 2026-11-15; the docs, the full `run_tests.sh`, the GPU check and the (H) checklist follow):**
  - **Call-site tests** (`251a1cd8`): `test_grid_aim_call_sites` 2/2 through `step_turrets` and `step_interceptors` on a real
    station. H1 (lifted 9.5 m) against a fighter crossing at 40 m/s, 2 km out, 11 degrees up: 6 hits of 9 shots; with `tk::aim(&at,
    ...)` reverted to `&f` (scratch copy only) 1 of 9, and the test fails. I5 against four bolts from 45 degrees up: the barrels it
    shows lie 0.04-0.15 degrees off each met pulse's line; with the stored angles reverted to the trunnion lead's 1.16-1.32, and it
    fails.
  - **`frame drop <km> [body]`** (`6832e781`): `WorldFrame.drop_toward` rides the body's own rail (`epsilon_iii`, or `epsilon` the
    star) with the scenario's origin placed so the player stands that high over it, at the rest velocity: the residual is the
    body's whole pull, the ship falls straight in, ALT counts down (live: 399 921 m 5 s after `frame drop 400`), the atmosphere's
    impact rule takes it, and the velocity rebase and REF work as anywhere. Refused outside the body's zone (D66: about 13 550 km
    over Epsilon III, 14.2 million km over the star) or where another body's zone wins, for a negative altitude and an unknown
    body. `info.md`; `test_frame_drop` 3/3 (400 km over the planet: 12.42 m/s down after 2 s against 12.52 from its pull; the star
    a million km up; the refusals).
  - **Jolt's job pool** (`cc4ec81d`): Godot's `JoltJobSystem` holds 2 048 jobs; Jolt makes jobs a step in proportion to the worker
    threads, and a job the main thread ran while waiting is freed only when its pool task comes round, so the test's 32 steps
    back to back a frame overflowed it on 12 threads and more (a bare Godot probe, one body: within 20 000 to 40 000 ticks; never at
    4 or 8 threads, or 1 to 16 steps a frame). `test_gravity_orbit`'s Jolt run takes one step a frame with headless's 6.9 ms frame
    sleep off for the run: the whole orbit in 61 s (was 75), worst 0.0606 m as before, no warning; no project setting changed.
  - **GPU tests** (`c6addeb3`): `test_sun_gpu` and `test_scaled_space_gpu` hide GUT's panel for each test; the planet's disc is
    measured top to bottom lit pixel (dark ground inside it read 409 of 412 px). At 960x540 they were 2/4 and 0/3, now 4/4 and
    3/3; 4/4 and 3/3 at the default window.
  - **Shard weights** (`5406d51c`): every file of 5 s or more from CI run 43790188947 (`test_cargo_carry_clearance` 600 -> 187 s,
    `test_station_wing_follow` 113, `test_station_pools` 10, `test_bot_crew` 5; files under 5 s dropped; `test_grid_aim_call_sites`
    at its local 37 s). That run's shards took 1 070 (plus Rust and smokes), 838, 1 280 and 1 241 s against 1 311 estimated
    each; the new plan estimates 1 234 s each. `check` green (416 files).
  - **Cargo notes**: T14's "Maintainer's choices (2026-11-14)" and "Done (as built, 2026-11-14 ...)" moved verbatim to M7-T30.
  - **Verified** in a `--staged --build-native` copy: compile check (867 scripts); GUT with `GUT_SLOW=1`, 17 files 101/101
    (`test_grid_aim_call_sites` 2, `test_frame_drop` 3, `test_gravity_orbit` 3, `test_free_fall` 7, `test_gravity_movers` 5,
    `test_flight_reference` 4, `test_frame_passes` 12, `test_scaled_space` 19, `test_console_command_list` 7, `test_heavy_cannon` 7,
    `test_station_grid` 9, `test_station_grid_threads` 1, `test_turret_kernel` 12, `test_parity_fixture` 3,

### M7-T33 The cargo pod

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** the freighters' cargo is moved by cargo pods, not maintenance bots (T34); the pod is a new craft.
- **Reference:** `references/EA_cargo_pod.stl` (gitignored, never committed). **Length 6 m** (maintainer). The 90% rule applies:
  top, side and front silhouette IoU >= 0.90 against the STL at 6 m and L/W/H within 10% (`tools/refview` compare).
- **What:** a generator in `tools/procgen` (both texture-detail sets, Earth Alliance colours read off the references, metallic not
  plastic), collision, LODs and the hardpoints T34 needs (where it grips a cargo block, its thrusters). Naming: the freighters' own
  cargo is already called pods in code (`FreighterCargo`, `FerryPod`, `src/ship/cargo_pods.gd`); the new craft gets its own name
  in code (for example `CargoPodCraft`, asset `cargo_pod_craft`) and "cargo pod" in the UI and docs.
- **Done when:** a GPU look sheet beside the STL views approved by the maintainer (H); the fidelity numbers; Python tests; committed.
- **Done (as built; the maintainer approved the look sheet on 2026-11-14, keeping the arm bands and the engine face's four bars):**
  - **The craft.** `tools/procgen/assets/cargo_pod_craft.py` and its style `styles/cargo_pod_craft.py`, described in
    `docs/design-references.md` ("Cargo pod"): a faceted blue cab (a wedge ahead of a taller cabin, a black window across the
    nose, two roof humps, the stern's ridge with the engine face between), two arms of stacked oval barrels, a wrist and a hand of
    two jaws. Colours from the show's zero-g cargo bay capture, white-balanced on the cab's pale roof; painted and worn metal
    (metallic 0.55 to 0.6, so the key light rather than the black sky shows it). 2 186 triangles, one 1 024 px atlas with the full
    and low texture sets (`settings.texture_detail`), levels of detail from Godot's importer as every hull's.
  - **Fidelity at 6 m, at rest:** top IoU 0.960, side 0.978, front 0.971; length 6.00/6.00 m, width 3.30/3.28 m, height
    2.10/2.07 m (`.venv/bin/python3 -I tools/refview/compare.py references/EA_cargo_pod.stl cargo_pod_craft --length 6
    --right=+y --up=+z --aft=+x`).
  - **The arms move (maintainer's question).** The glb is skinned (`core/glb.py` writes an optional skin, `TexturedPart.joint`
    binds a part whole to a joint): per arm the reach (the inner barrels, 1 m of travel, hidden inside the outer ones at rest), the
    wrist on its pivot drum and each jaw on its root. One surface, one draw call, hull and arms together. The manifest's `rig`
    lists the joints and their ranges; the rest pose is the STL's. `CargoPodPose` (pure maths: extend 0..1, wrist angle within
    ±45°, jaw open 1..0, and where a point on a hand ends up) and `CargoPodRig` (the node that builds the model and poses its
    skeleton; visuals only) are for T34.
  - **Hardpoints for T34** (`hardpoints.json`, body frame, at rest): `grips` (one per hand, between the jaws, with the bone it
    rides), `cargo_hold` (the jaws' tips, facing forward, 2.64 m across), `main_thruster` on the engine face, ten mirrored `rcs`
    with pilot tags, `nav_lights`, `cockpit`, the collision's boxes and capsules. The manifest's `collision` is one piece of 15
    convex shapes covering the mesh within 8 cm (the arms at rest).
  - **Tests:** `tools/procgen/tests/test_cargo_pod_craft.py` (21: files and both texture sets, size and frame, outward faces,
    metal, the skin and rig, hardpoints, collision cover, determinism, the glb skin round trip), `tests/unit/test_cargo_pod_pose.gd`
    (6), `tests/integration/test_cargo_pod_rig.gd` (2: skinned with nine bones in one surface, posing moves bones and grips).
  - **Captures:** `references/screenshots/T33 cargo pod/` (the look sheet beside the STL, and `T33 cargo pod poses.png`: rest,
    reaching, gripping).

### M7-T34 Cargo pods move the cargo

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** "replace the maintenance bots for cargo transport with the cargo pods ... the cargo pods handle
  all movement of cargo from ships to the cargo bay using the same logic as was created for the maintenance bots."
- **What:** T30's `CargoFerryPlan`, `CargoTrip`, `CargoFerryJob`, `FerryPod` and the carrying motion drive cargo pods (T33) instead
  of bots: the same crew counts (one per pod, two per block or tank), the same routes, clearances, tank turning, loading and the
  combat pause. Pods launch from and return to the cargo bay. The bots no longer carry cargo (`bots cargo` and the bot ferry go or
  move to the pods; keep the console commands working under the pods' names and update `info.md`). T30's tests carry over to the pods.
- **Done when:** the `traffic freighter ... show` runs work on all five Saturns with pods (maintainer's live check, H); the cargo
  tests pass in a `--staged` copy; committed.
- **Done (2026-11-14; accepted live by the maintainer, "t30 and t34 accepted", after the Saturn 4 transport and Saturn 8 tanker
  `show` runs with the crew bots out):**
  - **Where the ferry lives.** It left `BotFleet`: `CargoPodFleet` (a child of the station beside `Bots`) holds the `CargoPodCraft`s
    (T33's `CargoPodRig` on a node following its motion, arms posed each frame) on the frame, launched from and landing in the cargo
    bay's back wall. It reads the crew's alarm log and clock (`BotFleet.alert`, `now_s`), so hits, grid shots and weapons free stop
    pods and crew alike; a bare fleet keeps its own. `BotFleet` is the crew only (`ferry_bots`, `cargo` and the ferry's alarm slots
    gone; `build_on` lost its frame parent). `BotFerryMotion` is `CargoPodMotion` and `BotCargoScene` is `CargoPodScene`; T30's plan,
    routes, crew counts, tank turning, loading, combat pause and timing are as they were.
  - **The hold.** A pod holds an end face at its centre, hands on it and nose to the block: its centre 3.85 m off the face (grips 3.7
    m ahead with the arms out, a 0.15 m gap) and 0.40 m above the face's middle, turning with a tank as it rolls (T30's bot: 8 m).
    Its way out ends with a straight 10 m leg in along the block's axis; backing off it keeps its nose to the block for those 10 m.
    A two-pod block slides in until the far pod is past the wall; a carrying pod counts as hidden 4 m past it.
  - **The arms** (in the motion, pure): reach out (0.5 a second) with the jaws open over the last 20 m; the jaws shut over 1.25 s once
    it holds (inside the block's 3 s wait, so they are shut before it moves); held while carrying; letting go it stands still
    1.25 s while they open, then folds flying off (at once in a fight); loading starts already gripping; the T11 shuttle reaches out
    at the Saturn and folds going home.
  - **Console:** `pods [info|hide|show|cargo [n|off|view|bayview]]` and the `pods` provider (count, hidden, held, scene, jobs with each
    pod's phase and arms); `bots cargo` stays as an alias; `traffic freighter ...` unchanged; `info.md`, `docs/assets-station.md`.
  - **Tests** (from a `--staged` copy): `test_cargo_pod_motion` 7/7 (T30's `test_bot_ferry_motion` with the shuttle's arms),
    `test_cargo_pod_fleet` 5/5 (was `test_bot_ferry_fleet`; the crew's alarm), `test_ferry_pod` 10/10 (every tick a block moves, the
    pods holding it through the move have their jaws shut and arms out; the arms through a trip; loading starts gripping),
    `test_cargo_ferry_carry` 6/6 (hold at the reach, grips on the face's middle all the way, the last leg straight in),
    `test_cargo_pod_grip` 2/2 (the drawn rig's grip points are the plan's; both pods' hands stay on a rolling tank's faces within
    2 cm), `test_cargo_carry_clearance` 5/5 over the craft's body and hands (least: 10.6 m hull and 3.0 m Saturn on the way out,
    6.5 m from its block before the last leg; carrying 12.2 m hull, 14.0 m Saturn; blocks 6.0 m Saturn, 5.1 m in the bay's mouth),
    `test_cargo_ferry_clearance` 3/3, `test_cargo_ferry_plan` 5/5, `test_bot_fleet` 8/8, `test_station_traffic` 5/5,
    `test_bots_on_station` 10/10 (`pods cargo` and the `bots cargo` alias), `test_station_tour` 8/8, slow `test_freighter_cargo`
    3/3 (all five hulls unloaded by pods, loading, the combat pause with the drawn hands on the block's faces).

### M7-T35 Maintenance bots roam and repair

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** the bots become the station's maintenance crew, not cargo carriers.
- **Peace:** 3 to 6 bots out at a time, launched from the cargo bay and back to it, **one per area at a time and irregularly**
  (seeded per bot, never a shared RNG). Areas: along the spine, along the fins (vanes), along the drum (in the drum's turning frame,
  moving with it, so they are never struck), and out to the jumpgate and along its arm, and back.
- **Battle:** the cargo bay launches one bot per damaged area (damage sections, T06, and grid turrets, T07) up to about 12; each
  bot repairs **10 HP/s** on the area it works on; a knocked-out turret returns once repaired above zero; a lost section can be
  repaired back. Bots keep T11's alert behaviour where it still fits (getting clear of fire); repair rates and counts are tunables.
- **Rules:** sim time only; deterministic with 1 and 6 threads; the repair is per-event logic in GDScript unless it scales per
  tick with entity count (then native, CLAUDE.md); console commands and a state provider for the bots' jobs, listed in `info.md`.
- **Done when:** a live run shows the peace patrols (spine, fins, drum, gate) and a battle with bots repairing (maintainer, H);
  tests for the routes, the co-rotating drum frame, the one-per-area rule, the repair rate and the turret coming back; committed.
- **Done (as built; approved by the maintainer 2026-11-14 after a live run of the patrols and a battle):**
  - **The crew** (`src/props/`): `BotCrew` (who goes out, where and when; pure), `BotTripPlanner` (the trips from the roads),
    `BotTrip` (a trip's steps, the way back mirroring the way out, so a bot called home turns round where it is), `BotCrewMotion`
    (flies a trip: trapezoid speeds, the drum boarded from under it by ramping the bot's turn rate to the drum's and landing at a
    given drum angle, a transit that meets the moving jumpgate, all in the station's frame), `BotFrames` (the drum's angle and the
    gate's place, refreshed each tick), `BotRepairAreas` (the 11 damage sections and 23 grid mounts as repair areas, the most urgent
    first). `BotFleet` holds the pool of 12 (hidden in the cargo bay), the alarms and the console; T11's eight fixed loop bots and
    `BotMotion` are gone (they could not keep 3 to 6 out). Crew bots hang from the station's node, never its far-drawn pieces, and are
    not drawn past 20 km.
  - **Routes** (`data/station/bot_points.json`, the tunables too): the trunk out of the cargo bay past the bow, the high road at
    y 640 over the spine, the low road at y -500 under the drum; patrols along the spine, up or down a vane's outer face at x +-580
    (port and starboard, one of three vanes), along the drum's green zone (station z -1400..-750 and -100..550, riding at 355-360 m
    between the drum and the spine's underside) and out to the jumpgate (50 km at 250 m/s) and along an arm's outer face 75 m out.
    Work spots per section; a mount's along its up (22 m, interceptors 19 m; I6 from the side).
  - **Peace:** 6 areas, one bot each at a time; waits 60 to 400 s from a hash of the area and its trip number (no shared RNG);
    3 to 6 out (4 to 5 in a 4-minute headless run); no patrol starts while fighting is near the bay and a patrol near a fight turns
    home (T11's habit).
  - **Battle:** one bot per damaged area, knocked-out mounts and lost sections first, up to 12; a short pool calls patrols home;
    10 HP/s at the spot until whole (`StationSections.repair`, `Station.repair_section` with `section_repaired`,
    `GridMountHealth.heal`, `StationGrid.repair_some`); a mount comes back above zero, a lost section too and can be lost again (the
    `lost` signal fires again); a repair bot backs off 40 m while hits land within 100 m in the last 4 s.
  - **Cost (D78):** about 170 us a physics tick for the crew with six out (headless), so it stays in GDScript.
  - **Console:** `bots info|hide|show|send|peace|battle|follow|skip|goto|cargo` and the `bots` provider (`info.md`).
  - **Tests:** GUT `test_bot_trip` (3), `test_bot_crew_motion` (8: smooth flight, staying put on the turning drum, landing at a drum
    angle with no jump, getting off at the hold point, the drum held still, meeting the moving gate, turning home), `test_bot_crew`
    (10: 3 to 6 out one an area, seeded waits, fighting sends patrols home, battle counts up to 12, one bot an area, 10 HP/s, a turret
    back, a lost section repaired and lost again, a short pool, the duck), `test_bot_data` (3), `test_bot_fleet` (8),
    `test_bot_routes_clear_of_hull` (3: every patrol and repair trip 8 m from the drawn hull, the drum's riding line over a whole turn,
    the gate's arms; least 12.3 m at the bay's mouth), integration `test_bots_on_station` (10: the crew in the bay, patrols and the gate
    run, a bot staying put on the turning drum, rebases, a battle with L10 coming back, a lost section repaired and lost again, weapons
    free, ambient; the cargo scene's two tests unchanged).
  - **Sheet (H):** `references/screenshots/T35 bot patrols/` (spine, fins, drum, gate, a bot over L10, a bot under the drum).
  - **For T34:** the ferry is untouched (`ferry_bots`, `add_ferry_bot`, `remove_ferry_bot`, `clear_ferry`, `frame_parent`,
    `ferry_hidden_count`, the ferry's alarm and `bots cargo`); `test_bot_ferry_fleet` changed one assertion (the crew no longer hangs
    from the frame).

### M7-T36 The maintenance bot rebuilt to its reference

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-14):** "the top view of the maintenance bot in the game doesn't match the view" of
  `references/maintenance bot.webp` (gitignored; a four-view sheet: top, side, front and a three-quarter view).
- **What:** rebuild `tools/procgen/assets/maintenance_bot.py` from the sheet's orthographic views. There is no STL, so the views stand
  in for it: top, side and front silhouette IoU >= 0.90 against masks cut from the sheet at the same scale, and the proportions within
  10%. Keep the bot's size, its hardpoints and anything T35's crew and T11's alert use (thrusters, the arms' grip points, the light),
  and both texture-detail sets. The shells' blue, the gold bands and the painted numbers follow the sheet.
- **Done when:** a GPU look sheet with the reference views beside ours and the overlap numbers is approved by the maintainer (H);
  the bot's tests and T35's crew tests pass in a `--staged` copy; committed.
- **Done (as built; the maintainer approved the look sheet on 2026-11-14, accepting the symmetrised-mask bar):**
  - **Generator v2.** The shells are quarter eggs lofted from the plan and crest profiles measured off the sheet: flat inside and
    underneath, pointed at the nose, a 1.17 m gap down the middle. Between and under them: a truss deck, a beam with a dark slot under
    the shells, a tail block and a T-shaped mast. The arms hang outboard of the shells at x ±1.62 m and fold aft to the elbow, then
    forward to a two-prong claw. Under the middle: a sensor stalk with a lens box. All sizes are in `docs/design-references.md`
    "Maintenance bot".
  - **Kept.** The size: 4.65 m long; width 3.50 m against the sheet's 3.48, height 2.91 against 2.92 (ratios 1.000, 1.006, 0.997).
    The hardpoints are unchanged (bound, 3 lamps, no collision), there are 8 thrusters and 3 136 triangles. The game reads only the
    mesh. The lens is dimmed so it reads green on the GPU. The numbers are in the bands' gold, "9" and "6" seen from above as on the
    sheet. The bot never wrote texture-detail sets (only seamed parts do), so both settings use the full maps, as before.
  - **Overlap** (generator v1 in brackets): top 0.905 against the symmetrised mask, 0.844 raw (0.658); side 0.908 (0.443); front
    0.857 symmetrised, 0.801 raw (0.388).
  - **Why this bar.** The sheet is not left-right symmetric: the front is a perspective render and some truss details are one-sided.
    Against the raw masks a symmetric bot can reach at most 0.896 in the top view and 0.888 in the front (the mask scored against
    itself joined with its mirror image), so 0.90 raw cannot be met. Top and front are scored against that symmetrised mask, the
    best target for a symmetric shape, and the maintainer accepted the front at 0.857 against its 0.888 ceiling. The rule is written
    in `docs/design-references.md`.
  - **Look sheet** (GPU, Forward+): `references/screenshots/T36 maintenance bot/T36 look sheet.png` (gitignored), with the mask and
    scoring scripts beside it.
  - **Tests.** `test_maintenance_bot` gains the sheet's proportions. In the `--staged` copy: Python with PIL blocked 13 pass; the
    compile check is clean; the bot GUT files pass (bots_on_station 8, alert 5, data 2, ferry_fleet 4, ferry_motion 6, fleet 13,
    loop 9, loops_clear_of_hull 3, motion 8). T35's uncommitted crew tests passed with the new mesh in a working-copy scratch
    (crew 18, crew_motion 8, routes_clear_of_hull 3, trip 3).

### M7-T37 Radial menus on the left stick

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-15, after flying T27):** "change the radial button menu behavior to use the left controller stick when a
  radial menu is showing rather than the right controller stick."
- **What:** while a radial menu is open (the target menu held on B, and the comms menu), the **left stick** points at its entries
  instead of the right; the right stick goes back to what it does in flight (or nothing, if the menu locks it today). The left stick's
  flight input is held at neutral while the menu shows, so the ship does not turn or thrust from the same stick, and resumes cleanly
  when the menu closes (no jump from a stick still held). Deadzone, selection threshold and the existing confirm/cancel buttons stay
  as they are. Keyboard and mouse paths are unchanged (keyboard parity, the T27 rule). The controls screen and the pause menu's pad
  diagram, `docs/playtest.md` and the architecture input table say left stick for the menus.
- **Done when:** tests (the left stick picks entries in both menus, the right stick does not, the ship gets no left-stick input while
  a menu shows and none jumps in on close, keyboard and mouse unchanged); the maintainer's live run (H); committed.
- **Done (accepted live by the maintainer, 2026-11-15: "T37 passes"):** the six `radial_*` actions in `project.godot` moved from the right stick's axes (2, 3)
  to the left stick's (0, 1); the arrow keys, A/B and the mouse are as before. The game is paused behind both menus, so the right stick
  is simply not read and flies again the tick the menu closes (`InputAdapter.end_radial` no longer holds it over). The left stick's
  strafe is zero while a menu shows (`sample_radial` produces no flight command) and `end_radial` holds it over: `sample` strafes
  only after the **pad's** stick has been back inside the deadzone (`InputSource.get_stick_vector`, a pad-axes-only read, so a held
  strafe key Z/C/F/R is never held over). The pad diagram's left-stick row reads `L stick  Strafe / Menu pointer`. Tests: input map,
  adapter (left stick held over, near-centre not, right stick flies at once, keys not held over), the real `GodotInputSource` (left
  stick points a menu and the right does not; `get_stick_vector` ignores keys), pad diagram, and the target menu integration test now
  holds the left stick through a pick. Known: `test_target_preview.test_a_buoy_shows_its_wireframe_and_no_hull_bar` fails the same
  way at HEAD (not from this task). Left unguarded: a menu that opens while the left stick is already deflected (strafing while B is
  held) points at that direction at once; the maintainer flew it and did not object.

### M7-T38 AI ships avoid the station

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-15, flying the station showcase):** "i lost all 3 ally wingmen to the B5 station in a collision that
  could have been avoided."
- **What:** find why the wing flew into the station (T10's native avoidance against the station's surface: its range and look-ahead
  at the wing's speeds, the drum's rotation, the arms and fins as thin parts, formation-keeping overriding avoidance, following the
  player close past the hull) and make AI ships keep clear of the station and other large bodies whenever a collision is avoidable:
  enough look-ahead for their speed and turn rate, avoidance taking priority over formation and pursuit, a margin round thin parts,
  the rotating drum's surface speed. Native, per tick, 1 and 6 threads identical, parity recordings kept green (new recordings only
  with the reason stated).
- **Done when:** a test that reproduces the wing's loss (the showcase's wing following the player past the station) and passes;
  tests over the station's parts and the drum; the battle bench inside budget; the maintainer's live run (H); committed.
- **Done (as built, two rounds; contracts in `docs/architecture.md` §20.4; accepted live by the maintainer, 2026-11-15: "t38
  passes"):**
  - **Round 1 (all three wingmen lost).** Reproduced: a leader diving between the Cobra arms with ALPHA 2 to 4 on their
    finger-four slots lost all three on HEAD (rams at 43 to 66 m/s). Causes: (1) inside a turning hull's swept rings each ring
    pushed its own way out, so an arm's ring (196 to 246 m out) pushed a ship inward toward the arm's floor, into the 6.5 m above
    the drum's core; the pushes cancelled and the next arm struck it; (2) the slide and the pull-out looked only at the nearest
    skin point, so a ship sliding along the drum's forward face fell at an arm's ring beyond it at 70 to 95 m/s with only a
    sideways dodge against it; (3) pulling out, the wish still asked for the formation's whole speed along the skin. Fixes: the
    rings are one solid with the gaps narrower than two keep-off distances filled, and a ship among them has one way out, the
    shortest (inward only into a hole wide enough to fly); the slide and the pull-out use the skin point the ship has least room
    to stop short of (a turn and the burn at its speed), the nearest kept as a wall, and a part its way passes wide of is no
    fall; pulling out, the wish may change the ship's way along the skin by no more than the fall.
  - **Round 2 (maintainer: "3 passes between the cobra arms and 2 through both sets of fins ended up causing 2 of the 3 allies
    to be destroyed").** Reproduced on a pilot's tracks (turns of at most 24 m/s^2, the leader flying on while the wing
    rejoins): on round 1's build the fin passes lost two (ALPHA 2 and 3 rammed each other by the gap) and threading the arms
    against their turn lost one. Causes: (1) the slide worked out a fall again against a vane's edge the ship's way passes wide
    of: both wingmen braked 300 m short and slid to the gap's middle together (on HEAD too); (2) a thin plate met face on was
    missed, its nearest point being an edge; (3) a wish bent at 130 m/s toward the slot between the spine and the drum was
    limited only once the velocity followed it; (4) wingmen left behind rejoin at 200 m/s and cut through the leader's turn.
    Fixes: the ship's stopping path is swept against every volume (a turning hull's rings) grown by the keep-off, and the face it
    meets is a candidate (`surface::path_entry`); the slide takes the fall `skin_points` found; a wish leading at a face it could
    not stop short of makes that face a wall (`wish_wall`); a wingman whose slot lies inside or within 55 m of a big hull's skin,
    or across one, **trails**: it flies the leader's way (a point every 0.25 s for 16 s, kept relative to the hull), to the newest
    point it reaches in a straight line that is at least 0.5 s plus its place in the formation behind (so the wing goes in
    file), else the attack's detour, and rejoins when the slot is clear; near a big hull a friendly ship it could not stop short
    of is a wall to its wish, and one within 50 m is pushed off (`kept_apart`). Away from big hulls the formation is as it was.
  - **Tests.** GUT `test_station_wing_follow` (9, a slow file, about 2 min): down the drum, over the bow and the spine, round the
    arms, along the fins, the dive between the arms, three laps threading the arms with and against their turn, both fin sets
    twice, and the dive threaded against delayed and one thread against six (rows and commands the same bits every tick).
    Wingmen lost, HEAD / round 1 / round 2: the dive 3 / 0 / 0; the arms with their turn 1 / 0 / 0 (nearest 55 m); against it
    1 / 1 / 0 (39 m); the fins 3 / 2 / 0 (50 m); the other four 0 / 0 / 0 (36 m or more). Clean too at 100 and 140 m/s round
    the arms and 85 and 115 m/s through the fins; 200 m/s straight into the waist is beyond a Starfury's stop (1.2 km needed,
    650 m given). Rust 203 (+12): the arms' ring pushes out, a tunnel's hole is still a way out, the urgent part beyond a
    nearer face, a corner passed wide, the pull-out's capped change, a plate's face found past its edge, a ring's rim, end and
    hole, the face-on pull-out, the wish wall, a slot inside the hull trails, the trail kept through a rebase, no closing on a
    wingmate near a hull. The station, traffic, launch, parity and formation GUT files pass unchanged; **no recording moved**
    (none holds a big hull).
  - **Bench** (`bench_quick.sh battle`, back to back in one copy): 500 ships tick median 5.34 to 5.54 ms, p99 8.24 to 8.81 ms,
    AI 0.23 to 0.24 ms; every count inside budget (the layout has no big hull). Near a big hull an avoidance call costs more
    (round 1: 3.0 against 2.9 us off the skin, 9.8 against 3.1 us within the keep-off).
  - **Open (maintainer):** the station's raid balance is out of band on HEAD after T41 and T43 (raid mean 77.9 s against 90 to
    120, the capital lost on seed 1); with T38 the raid mean is 71.9 s and the capital unchanged; T43 is reconciling it.
    **Note (M7-T40 round 2, 2026-11-15):** those figures came from a copy missing the `grid_heavy_cannon` asset, so the heavy
    cannons fired without their own trunnion and barrel; T43's fix round gave `StationGrid` the gun's own figures. HEAD 6472d504
    with the asset: raid mean 88.9 s (80.8, 95.8, 89.9), the capital destroyed at 122 s on every seed. T40 round 2 settles it.
    Ship-to-ship dodging in plain formation away from big hulls stays weak (a wingman rejoining fast can ram a stopped leader);
    left as it is for now.

- **Accepted (maintainer, 2026-11-15, round 2 live):** "t38 passes". Committed 1972e195, pushed with 6472d504; CI green (run 43780325834).

### M7-T39 Fix: the console key

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-15):** "` doesn't activate the console anymore which must be fixed" (seen in the station showcase from a
  copy of HEAD plus T18's lights).
- **What:** find what stops `DebugConsole._input` from opening on the console key (physical KEY_QUOTELEFT): an earlier `_input`
  handler or focused Control consuming the event, the `console_enabled` setting, the window/display driver's key codes after the
  maintainer's OS upgrade, or a scenario that drops the console. Fix it at the cause; a test that a real console-key event opens the
  console in the main scene and in the station showcase.
- **Done when:** the key opens the console live (H); the test; committed.
- **Done:** the cause was the key's physical code. Wayland reports the ISO key left of `1` as `KEY_SECTION` (167) while the layout types a
  backtick on it (logical keycode 96); `DebugConsole._is_console_key` only knew `KEY_QUOTELEFT`. The maintainer's key press in a short
  window gave `phys=167 key=96`; nothing else consumed the key. `DebugConsole.is_console_physical()` accepts both, and `bind` refuses
  `key:Section` too. `test_console_key_in_play.gd` (4 tests: free flight and the showcase, ANSI and ISO key) plus a `bind` refusal and an
  InputMap check; 2 of the 4 fail without the fix. The live recheck (H) comes with the maintainer's next window.

### M7-T40 Council review fixes

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-15: "use council to do a full code review of m7"):** three reviewers read `2b607df4..90fa7f7a`; the
  controller checked every finding against the code and all nine below hold. Scores, round 1: Opus 8/10, GPT-6 Luna 6/10 (no
  findings), DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 8/10.
- **What (native, Opus):**
  1. P2: the interceptor batteries shoot through the station's own hull and below their arc (`turret_rows.rs` `step_interceptors`,
     `grid_kernels.rs` `incoming`): a battery takes only a projectile its line reaches unblocked (`line_blocked`) and inside its
     elevation limits, as the guns do (AC6, "the station's hull blocks its fire").
  2. P3: a civilian near the station is scared by the station's own grid fire (`brain_behaviours.rs`, the M7-T29 close-fire check):
     a big hull's (a row with a surface) fire does not scare.
  3. P3: `time_to_hull` is swept once per battery for every bolt; once per bolt per tick is enough.
  4. P3: `turret_snapshot_into` allocates and sorts the surfaces' keys every tick; reuse a scratch buffer as `grid_surface_rows` does.
- **What (GDScript and Python, Sonnet):**
  5. P3: `StationTraffic._release` zeroes a pooled civilian's transponder and nothing restores it; `ShipPool.acquire` gives each
     type its own range back.
  6. P3: `FreighterCargo` emits `finished` (and removes the crew) every tick after an unload ends; once only.
  7. P3: a failed `LaunchDirector.launch_wing` leaves the twelve parked hulls in the wing (and a new empty wing); roll both back.
  8. P3: `ScenarioLoader._launch_bay` accepts a door outside 1 to 6; refuse it as its error text says.
  9. P3: `scripts/python_shards.py check` cannot fail (it checks its own assignment); make it fail when a test module under
     `tools/procgen/tests` (subfolders too) is in no shard.
- **Done (GDScript/Python, items 5-9):** 5. `ShipPool.default_broadcast_range_m(type)` is the one source of a type's transponder range
  (ally 100 km, civilian 15 km, raider none); `prepare` and `acquire` both use it, so a Saturn the traffic silenced is heard again
  by its next taker (`test_ally_pool`). 6. `FreighterCargo` tells `finished` and removes its crew once per job (`_told_finished`,
  reset by `start`; `test_freighter_cargo_finish`). 7. A failed `LaunchDirector.launch_wing` takes the hulls out of the wing again
  (new `WingDirector.leave`, which also lifts a leader) and drops a wing that call made when it is left empty
  (`test_launch_bay`). 8. `ScenarioLoader._launch_bay` accepts only a whole door number 1 to 6 (3.0 passes, JSON gives floats; 0, 7,
  2.5 and "3" are refused with the existing text; `test_scenario_loader`). Wing names stay unchecked there: the loader would have to
  read `LaunchDirector.WINGS`, a game-layer constant. 9. `python_shards.py check` compares the shards with an rglob of
  `tools/procgen/tests` (new `on_disk`), so a module in a subfolder, or anything `discover` misses, fails it with exit 1
  (`test_ci_shards`, on a temporary folder). Each new test fails with its fix removed. Counts from the staged scratch copy (HEAD plus
  these changes, GUT_SLOW=1): 861 scripts compile; test_freighter_cargo_finish 1, test_scenario_loader 40, test_ally_pool 20
  (2 scripts), test_ship_pool 21, test_launch_bay 12, test_station_traffic 5, test_freighter_cargo 4, test_launch_sequence 25,
  test_launch_cut 7, test_wing* 57 all pass; test_ci_shards 7 pass (with PIL blocked).
- **Done (native, items 1-4):** 1. A battery shoots only what it can reach: `grid_kernels::choose_in_reach` keeps `choose`'s
  order and asks the would-be winner only (as the guns' pick does) to lie inside the battery's arc (elevation from its pivot at
  least `min_elevation`) with its line from `tk::line_start` entering no big hull (`line_blocked`). Fire it cannot shoot still sets
  the latch (§20.3: it was fired at the station). `choose` stays for the order's own test only. Test
  `a_battery_takes_only_a_projectile_inside_its_arc_that_the_hull_does_not_hide` (a hidden bolt and a low one refused, one above
  taken; the old `choose` takes the hidden one). 2. The M7-T29 close-fire check skips a shooter with a surface: the station's grid
  fire neither scares nor provokes (`the_stations_own_grid_fire_does_not_frighten_its_traffic`, fails with the fix removed; a
  friendly fighter's bolt on the same pass still frightens). Limit: `close_fire_from` gives the earliest bolt row's shooter, so a
  grid bolt earlier in row order hides a non-hostile ship's close bolt for that think; filtering inside it belongs to `kernels.rs`
  (held by T38). 3. `incoming` takes `hull_times`, one per bolt (turret scratch, `NOT_SWEPT` each tick): a bolt is swept against
  the hull once a tick for all nine batteries, same values (`the_batteries_sweep_each_bolt_against_the_hull_once_a_tick`).
  4. `sorted_surface_bodies` reuses the turrets' `surface_bodies` buffer for both the job's copy and `grid_surface_rows`
  (`turret_snapshot_into` now takes `&mut self`). Architecture §20.3 (the batteries) and the M7-T29 civilian bullet say so.
  Counts from the staged scratch copy (70ff64b3 plus these changes; items 5-9 touch no native or grid file): Rust 183 pass;
  860 scripts compile; GUT_SLOW=1, 16 files, 81/81 pass (test_station_grid, _grid_threads, _ai, _traffic, _threads,
  test_grid_mount_health, test_turret_kernel, the parity files test_parity_fixture, test_flight_parity_fight,
  test_weapon_rows_battle, test_pilot_kernels_in_play, test_brain_tolerance, test_targeting_on_read, test_hit_scale, test_sim_core,
  test_core_state). No recording changed. Balance (`test_station_grid_balance`, before = 70ff64b3, after = the fix):
  - raid: before mean 108.1 s (102.5, 98.8, 123.0), shot down 120, 108, 124; after mean 109.4 s (122.1, 102.1, 104.1), shot down
    109, 103, 81. Pass.
  - capital: before shot down 852, 858, 858 (41 missiles each), station lost 4373, 4426, 4425, worst section 64, 65, 65 %; after
    shot down 334, 364, 369 (34, 37, 37 missiles), lost 6950, 6548, 6409, worst section 100 % on every seed. **Fails** "no section
    lost". The cruiser fires at the bow from 300 to 700 m up and 2.2 to 2.7 km ahead; most batteries had been taking those bolts
    below their 10-degree-down arc or through the frame. Arc check alone: 556 to 560 shot down; line check alone: 391 to 423; both
    at a 0.5 s interval instead of 1 s: 605, 754, 806, worst section 100, 100, 94 % (raid mean 105.0 s), still failing. So the
    D75 layout cannot hold the bow against the capital once the shots are honest. **Open, for the maintainer:** move or add bow
    batteries, widen their arc, or lower the test's bar. `test_turret_balance` (no station) 2/2 pass.
- **Round 2 (council, 2026-11-15):** scores Opus 8/10, GPT-6 Luna 8/10, DeepSeek 9/10. The controller checked four findings
  against 6472d504; all hold, all fixed:
  1. P2 (Opus): a heavy cannon aimed from its pivot but fired from its trunnion 9.5 m up, so the bolt flew parallel to the aimed
     line about 9 m off it and missed a fighter (hit radius 1 m). `turret_kernels::raised` gives a lifted gun's frame at its
     trunnion: the lead, the gate and the shot all start there (and a drum gun's skin speed); an unlifted mount keeps its own
     frame, so every other mount is bit-identical and no recording moved. Rust
     `a_raised_gun_aims_from_the_trunnion_it_fires_from`: a 2 km crossing fighter at 10, 20 and 30 degrees up is now passed at
     0.00 m; aimed from the base the same shot passed 9.36, 8.93 and 8.23 m off.
  2. P3 (DeepSeek): a battery's barrels pointed along the trunnion's lead while the pulse left a corner muzzle on that muzzle's
     own lead, visibly sideways up close. `grid_kernels::battery_shot` lays the barrels, leads again from the firing muzzle and
     shows the mount along the pulse's line (clamped to its arc); the pulse's start, path and hits are unchanged (the battery's
     trunnion sits on its yaw axis, so the shown azimuth moves no later shot). Rust
     `a_battery_shows_its_barrels_along_the_pulse_it_fires` (20 m off: under 0.01 degrees; the old angles over 1 degree off).
  3. P3 (Opus): the batteries' smaller meeting flash lasted one frame (`ImpactFlash.advance` reset its scale).
     `ImpactFlash.create` takes a size for the flash's whole life; `StationGrid._on_intercepted` passes `MEET_FLASH_SCALE`; hull
     hits unchanged. GUT `test_a_meeting_flash_keeps_its_smaller_size_as_it_grows` (`test_impact_flash`).
  4. P3 (Opus): `traffic.reserved_ms` (M7-T13) added to info.md's `traffic` provider row.
  - Left as is: `TurretRows._spend` re-spends pulse targets the core already marked spent (harmless).
