### M7-T44 Fix: two suns near the star

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-15, flying the T38 build):** "from the 100000 frame orbit around epsilon, i see 2 suns, not one".
  The maintainer tried `frame orbit` at 10 000, 100 000, 1 000 000 and 10 000 000 km above the star (Epsilon, radius 511 000 km);
  at 100 000 km two suns show.
- **What:** find which two things draw a sun there (the scaled-space star at its true place, the sky's sun disc or panorama, the lens
  flare, the key light's direction, a far-dot or impostor) and why they part near the star (parallax: one drawn at infinity along the
  key light's direction while the other sits at the star's true place, which is no longer at infinity this close; or the star's size
  past the far plane). Fix it at the cause so one sun shows at every distance from the star, the planet's views unchanged.
- **Done when:** a test of the sun's place and count at several distances (the star's true direction and the key light, flare and sky
  agree); screenshots from the four distances; the maintainer's look (H); committed.
- **Done (fix, 2026-11-15; the maintainer's look (H) open):** the cause was the star's own shader, not a second element. Hiding the
  scaled-space layer (`scaled hide`) left no sun at all, and the layer draws one sphere for the star. That sphere is `SunLook.GLOW_SCALE`
  (10) disc radii, so closer than 10 star radii (5.11 million km) the camera sits inside it. `scaled_star.gdshader` measured a pixel's
  distance from the disc's centre as the sine of its angle off the centre over the sine of the disc's angular radius, and the sine folds
  back past 90 degrees: from inside the sphere its far side drew a second disc, exactly as large, straight opposite the star (seen at
  10 000, 100 000 and 1 000 000 km; 10 000 000 km is outside the sphere, so one sun there). The sun's direction, the key light and the
  flare's aim already agreed (all from `ScaledPlacement` via `sun_direction`). Fix: past 90 degrees the coordinate keeps growing
  (2 - sine), so the far side reads as faint corona, never disc; `SunLook.disc_radii` holds the same law for the tests. From outside the
  sphere no drawn pixel is more than 90 degrees off, so the planet's views and every far view are unchanged. Close in, the corona's haze
  still tints the sky behind (as before the fix); its look is (H). Tests: `test_sun_look.gd` (the sine law on the near side; from 10 000
  km to 0.6 AU the coordinate grows all the way round and only the true disc is under 1), `test_scaled_space.gd`
  `test_one_sun_from_near_the_star_to_the_planet` (4 star distances, 400 and 1 000 km above Epsilon III and the station's rail: the
  drawn star, the placement, the key light and the flare's aim agree, only the cap round the star reads as disc, one star sphere), and
  `test_sun_gpu.gd` `test_close_to_the_star_there_is_no_second_sun_opposite_it` (GPU read-back 100 000 km up, the disc alone: white
  toward the star, dark behind; fails on the old shader with 3.0 behind, passes after). Staged copy: compile check 864 scripts; GUT
  (GUT_SLOW=1) test_sun_look 9/9, test_lens_flare_aim 6/6, test_scaled_placement 10/10, test_flash_flare 4/4, test_scaled_space 19/19,
  test_world_frame 18/18, test_environment 19/19, test_post_effects 20/20; on the RX 6950 XT the new GPU test passes, while
  `test_sun_gpu.gd`'s disc-height and colour asserts and all 3 of `test_scaled_space_gpu.gd` fail the same on HEAD (they sample the
  middle column, under GUT's own panel). Screenshots before and after (6 views at 10 000, 100 000, 1 000 000 and 10 000 000 km above the
  star and 400 and 1 000 km above Epsilon III): `references/screenshots/M7-T44 two suns/`.

- **Accepted (maintainer, 2026-11-15, live window from debb2669):** "just one as far as i could see". Pushed with 6472d504; CI green (run 43780325834).

### M7-T45 Fix: the radar's dots vanish after several waves

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-15, the M4 checklist's M4-T36 check):** "The radar turns into stalks only after several waves of raiders in
  the combat sandbox. no more dots."
- **What:** reproduce in the combat sandbox over several waves (`play sandbox`, `wave next` to hurry them), find why the dots stop
  drawing while the stalks go on (M4-T36's batched draw: a buffer, count or index that grows, wraps or goes stale as ships die and
  pooled ships come back; the native `ScsRadarPlot` columns), fix it at the cause; a test that runs many waves' worth of contacts
  coming and going and checks every contact still gets its dot.
- **Done when:** the test; the maintainer's look (H); committed.
- **Done (code part, 2026-11-16):** not a count, buffer or cull rect of ours, and not the roll: a Godot 4.7.2 bug. The first time a
  multimesh is written with motion vectors on (motion blur, TAA, FSR 2.2), Godot swaps its GPU buffer for one twice the size and
  frees the old one, but keeps the 2D renderer's cached binding (`uniform_set_2d`) to the freed buffer; from then on that multimesh
  draws nothing and `draw_list_bind_uniform_set: Parameter "uniform_set" is null` prints every frame, until it is resized. The radar's
  dot multimeshes grow early in a run (which healed them); once they stop growing, any later switch-on of motion vectors (the
  maintainer's console history: `settings.motion_blur on`, `upscale fsr2 quality`) leaves only the stalks, which are not a multimesh.
  Reproduced on the GPU in the sandbox (blur off, dots; `set settings.motion_blur true`, stalks only, the error each frame). Fix: the
  dots go through `RadarDotBatch` (`src/hud/radar_dot_batch.gd`), which notes the GPU buffer each draw used and, when Godot moved it
  under a multimesh it did not resize, resizes the multimesh once (to 0 and back), so the next draw binds the new buffer; the batched
  draw and its cost are unchanged (one buffer-id read per multimesh a frame). Tests: `tests/integration/test_radar_dots_gpu.gd` (GPU;
  pending headless): contacts come and go from 4 to 300 with the ship rolled through 0-360 degrees, then a motion-vector effect comes on
  and the run goes on, every frame's dots read back from the GPU; worst share drawn 0.00 with the rebuild disabled, passes with it;
  `tests/unit/test_radar_dot_batch.gd`. GPU runs with the fix: sandbox chase and cockpit octagon after blur on, two waves and FSR 2.2,
  and `bench battle 300` with blur switched on after the arrivals: dots drawn, no `uniform_set` error
  (`references/screenshots/M7-T45 radar dots/`). Likely the same cause as T49's error spam (the radar draws in every bench fight).
- **Accepted (maintainer, 2026-11-16):** HUD and radar dots held through a `bench battle 1000` run on fd45acba.

### M7-T46 Fix: FSR 2.2 too blurry

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-15, the M4 checklist's M4-T40 check):** "FSR2 is showing very very blurry results" (`upscale fsr2
  quality`, at the maintainer's 4K display).
- **What:** measure what FSR 2.2 gets (the internal size against the window's, `upscale` state, the sharpness, the motion vectors
  from the bolts, the sky and the hulls, the jitter, mip bias, TAA/AA interplay, `RenderScale` or the window's own scale on Wayland
  at 4K) against Godot's expected setup, find why it is far blurrier than its preset should be, fix it at the cause; GPU captures of
  `native` against `fsr2 quality` still and in motion.
- **Done when:** captures that show the fix; the maintainer's look (H); committed.
- **Done (code; 2026-11-16, the look is (H)):** the blur was the motion blur, not FSR 2.2's setup. Scale, internal size (2 560x1 440
  of a 3 840x2 160 fullscreen window at Quality, 1 600x900 of a 2 400x1 350 window), sharpness and the AA hold were all as M4-T40
  meant, and FSR 2.2 alone was as sharp as native in still and moving captures. The maintainer plays with `motion_blur` on, and
  under FSR 2.2 Godot 4.7 runs a separate motion pass: only what moved this frame writes a motion vector, and everything still is
  cleared to a (-1, -1) mark that FSR 2.2 works out from depth (`render_forward_clustered.cpp`, `ffx_fsr2_callbacks_glsl.h`). TAA,
  FSR 1.0 and native write every vector, so the mark appears only under FSR 2.2. `MotionBlurEffect` read the mark as a real vector
  and smeared every still hull (the player's ship at rest, the station, rocks) diagonally by the full 6 % cap (86 internal pixels at 4K Quality), even with
  the camera still. A read-back probe found (-1, -1) at every still hull pixel under `fsr2` at every preset and zero under native,
  TAA and FSR 1.0. Fix: `motion_blur.glsl` treats the mark as Godot's FSR 2.2 does and works the still pixel's vector out from its
  depth and the camera's move since the last frame. Moving things and the sky are unchanged, and native and FSR 1.0 never see the
  mark. New GPU test `test_motion_blur_upscaled_gpu` (2 tests: a still box keeps its rims under FSR 2.2 with the blur at full
  strength, and a camera slide blurs it as native does; both fail on the old shader, with rims 0.82 against 0.92 or more). Captures
  in `references/screenshots/M7-T46 FSR2/`: before `sbs_blur_on_still.png`, `before_w1080_fsr2_quality_still.png`; after
  `after_sb_*`, `after_w1080_*`; side by side `compare_still_native_before_after.png`, `compare_w1080_still_before_after.png`,
  `compare_turn_native_after.png`. Found on the way, not fixed: in `bench battle 300`, every frame after TAA or FSR 2.2 has been on
  prints Godot's `draw_list_bind_uniform_set: Parameter "uniform_set" is null` (about 55 a second, and it goes on after switching
  back to native or SMAA). It is not in the sandbox, and it is not this blur.
- **Accepted (maintainer, 2026-11-16):** "Works now" (m4-025, `upscale fsr2 quality` in `bench battle 500` on 2ae6876d).

### M7-T47 Friendly fire and motion blur on by default

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-15):** "i did want friendly fire and motion blur on. these should be defaults." Found when the controller's
  local full run failed in eight files: the maintainer's saved `settings.cfg` (`friendly_fire=true`, `motion_blur=true`) leaked into the
  tests, which read the player's saved settings; with an empty settings folder all 82 tests in those files pass.
- **What:**
  1. `SettingsSchema`: `friendly_fire` and `motion_blur` default to true (the settings menu, `docs/playtest.md` and info.md say so).
  2. The test suite never reads or writes the player's saved settings: GUT runs start from the schema's defaults in memory (or a
     settings file of their own), whatever `user://settings.cfg` holds; a test proving it.
  3. Tests that relied on the old defaults set what they need explicitly: the parity recordings in `tests/fixtures/` were recorded with
     friendly fire off, so their tests pin it off and stay bit-identical (no re-recording); the friendly-bolt tests (`test_hit_resolver`,
     `test_station_grid`, `test_civilian_attacked`, the post-effects and anti-aliasing tests, `test_settings_schema`) state their setting.
  4. The balance tests (`tests/balance_suite.txt`) run with the new defaults: report before/after; a balance band that no longer holds
     is reported to the maintainer with the numbers, not retuned silently.
- **Done when:** the defaults changed; the suite green locally with the maintainer's own settings file in place and on CI; the balance
  numbers recorded; committed.
- **Done (as built, 2026-11-16; CI and the maintainer's read pending):**
  - **Defaults:** `friendly_fire` and `motion_blur` default to true in `SettingsSchema` (the settings menu reads the schema);
    `docs/playtest.md` and architecture §7.5, §12, D31 and the M3 settings notes say so (info.md states no defaults).
  - **Isolation:** `Settings.test_run` is on under GUT (`gut_cmdln.gd` on the command line) or with `SCS_TEST_SETTINGS=1`, which a
    GUT run sets for the Godot processes it starts (`test_flight_parity_fight`'s fights) and `run_tests.sh` exports for its smokes.
    Then `revert` and `save` leave the player's files (`player_config_path`, `user://settings.cfg`, and `user://gamecontrollerdb.txt`)
    alone: the values start at the schema's defaults and `save` keeps them in memory. A test with a file of its own works as before.
    `test_settings_isolation` saves odd values to a stand-in player file the way the game does, then shows a test run starts at the
    defaults, ignores the file's pad calibration and never rewrites the file (the real file is never touched).
  - **Tests:** the recorded-fight tests pin friendly fire off and stay bit-identical, nothing re-recorded (`test_brain_tolerance`,
    `test_flight_parity_fight` in its child fights, `test_weapon_rows_battle`, `test_pilot_kernels_in_play`, `test_bolt_rows`,
    `test_core_state`, `test_driver_step`, `test_row_commands`, `test_sim_core`; `test_hit_scale` already did). The friendly-bolt
    tests state theirs (`test_hit_resolver`, `test_civilian_attacked`, `test_station_grid`'s own-side bolt: off); `test_post_effects`
    checks blur on by default and off asking for nothing; `test_settings_schema` checks both defaults.
  - **Suite** (whole GUT run, `GUT_SLOW=1`, four processes, HEAD plus the staged change): with the maintainer's real `settings.cfg`
    in place, 4141 tests, 1 failure; with `XDG_DATA_HOME` on a copy of it, 4141 tests, 1 failure. Each failure was Jolt's "job system
    exceeded the maximum number of jobs" warning under the load of four runs (`test_low_orbit_flight`, `test_gravity_orbit`); both
    files pass when run alone (8 of 8). His file's SHA-256 is unchanged after both runs. Rebuilt on M7-T48's commit, the files
    this task and M7-T48 touched pass with his settings in place: 237 of 237 (with the re-recorded `weapon_rows_battle`).
  - **Balance** (`GUT_BALANCE=1`, friendly fire off, then on): every band reads the same. Turrets: Saturn vs 2 raiders 2/1/3/1/1
    wins of 10 per hull, vs 3 raiders 0 of 10, both times. Grid vs 12 raiders: 3 of 3 wins at the same times, the station losing
    258/508/384 hp against 252/497/378. Grid vs a capital: identical. Interceptor proof 1.76 times against 1.78. The dogfight
    (seed 3 ends with 3 raiders left, not 5) and the M3 sandbox (identical) stay pending, as before: under the maintainer's review.
- **Accepted (maintainer, 2026-11-16):** on fd45acba; CI's factions smoke fixed in 3f82d079.

### M7-T48 The benchmarks and tests use the generated asteroids

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-15):** "update the 1000 ship and other tests to use the accurate asteroids rather than the spherical stand
  ins." The bench layouts' asteroid slab (`BenchLayout.rocks`, `StandinDirector.add_asteroids`) builds M4's `AsteroidField` stand-in:
  grey spheres in one multimesh, one collision sphere per rock. M5-T29's `AsteroidBelt` draws the generated `asteroids` shapes (one
  multimesh per shape) and collides each rock as its shape's one to three spheres, through the same `StaticRows` and `RockBodies`.
- **What:** the bench's rocks become generated asteroids (the layout's sizes and places kept, a shape and orientation per rock from
  the bench's seed), drawn and collided as `AsteroidBelt` does; the tests that build stand-in fields (`test_rocks_and_mines`,
  `test_standins`, `test_rock_bodies`, `test_collision_layers`, `test_velocity_rebase`) use the generated rocks where they test rock
  behaviour; the stand-in `AsteroidField` removed when nothing needs it (CLAUDE.md: no fallback kept). Any parity recording that holds
  rocks is re-recorded only with the reason stated. The bench's cost before/after (`bench battle 1000`, `bench typical`, the
  `scripts/bench_quick.sh` sweep) inside the M4 budgets.
- **Done when:** no bench or test builds sphere stand-in rocks; the suite green; the bench numbers recorded; the maintainer's look at a
  `bench battle 1000` (H); committed.
- **Done (code part, 2026-11-16):** `AsteroidBelt.build_rocks` is the one builder: the belt's `build(layout)` and the bench's
  `StandinDirector.add_asteroids` both call it, so the bench's rocks are drawn a multimesh per shape and collide as their shape's
  spheres, registered in `StaticRows` and streamed by `RockBodies` exactly as the belt's are (scan, hit sweep, rams, avoidance,
  rebases, drift). `BenchLayout.rocks` keeps every place and size and adds a shape and turn per rock from the rock's own hash
  (`AsteroidBelt.seeded_look`, no shared generator). `AsteroidField` is removed. Tests: `GeneratedRocks` (tests/fixtures) gives
  round rocks (a generated shape that collides as one sphere, sized so that sphere has the radius asked for: the hit and ram tests
  that aim at a rock's face keep their numbers, rock i is sphere i) and mixed rocks (any shape and turn: the 1 000-rock, static-shift,
  rebase and velocity-rebase tests, which now count spheres, about 2.1 a rock); `test_bench_layout` checks the seeded looks.
- **Recording:** `weapon_rows_battle.json` re-recorded, the only recording with rocks (its 100-ship battle holds the bench's 1 000):
  HEAD before the change gave 780, 380, 144, 145, 25, 1 against the stored 779, 380, 144, 145, 25, 1; with the generated rocks the
  battle gives 704, 321, 132, 146, 25, 1 on two runs (capitals' hits 15 % down, outside its 10 % gate). The reason is in
  `tests/fixtures/parity/README.md`.
- **Cost** (headless, seed 1, pinned to 0-11, before = HEAD and after in scratch copies, run back to back; load averages 2 to 9 from
  parallel agents): `bench battle 1000` 30 s: tick median 6.26 → 6.37 ms, p99 9.85 → 9.96 ms; the rams' stage 0.61 → 0.77 ms mean
  (about two sphere rows a rock now), the hit sweep 0.64 → 0.63 ms. `bench typical 200`: 3.53 → 3.51 ms, p99 5.33 → 5.51 ms;
  `typical 300`: 3.76 / 11.13 ms (load 8) → 3.33 / 5.29 ms. `scripts/bench_quick.sh <label> battle typical`: the budget held up to
  300 (battle) and 400 (typical) ships before and after (one after run, typical 300, p99 15.8 ms under load; 5.4 ms repeated).
  Windowed `bench battle 300` (960x540, RX 6950 XT): draw calls median 277 both, max 303 → 314; GPU time 0.51 → 1.27 ms median;
  VRAM max 871 → 932 MB. All inside the M4 budgets (median 8 ms, p99 12 ms). Left: the maintainer's look at a `bench battle 1000` (H).
- **Accepted (maintainer, 2026-11-16):** the generated asteroids in `bench battle 1000` on fd45acba.

### M7-T49 Fix: the `uniform_set` error spam

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-15):** "I'm seeing ERROR: Parameter "uniform_set" is null. (servers/rendering/rendering_device.cpp:5787)
  spamming in the console while testing." The controller could not reproduce it in free flight, the showcase, TAA, FSR or blur toggles,
  window resizes or a short `bench battle 300`; T46 did: in `bench battle 300`, once TAA or FSR 2.2 has been switched on,
  `draw_list_bind_uniform_set: Parameter "uniform_set" is null` prints about 55 times a second and keeps going after switching back to
  native or SMAA; not in the sandbox.
- **What:** find which draw binds a null uniform set (a compute or compositor effect, the bolts' or the far dots' multimesh with motion
  vectors, the motion-blur pass, a shader whose texture was freed when the 3D buffers were rebuilt for the new mode) and fix it at the
  cause; a test that switches TAA and FSR 2.2 on and back in a bench-sized fight and finds no such error.
- **Done when:** the test; the maintainer sees no spam (H); committed.

- **Resolved by M7-T45 (3892c82d, controller's check 2026-11-16):** the same Godot bug (a multimesh's 2D uniform set left pointing at a
  freed buffer once motion vectors come on) made the radar's dots vanish and printed this error every frame. On HEAD with the T45 fix,
  T46's repro (`bench battle 300 120 arrive=placed`, then `aa taa`, `upscale fsr2 quality`, back to `aa smaa` and `upscale native`,
  `blur on`, `blur off`) printed no `uniform_set` error and no other error. No separate change.
- **Accepted (maintainer, 2026-11-16):** no `uniform_set` error in the live `bench battle 1000` run on fd45acba (0 in its log).

### M7-T50 Fix: asteroids and far capitals vanish after a stutter

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-16, a bench fight):** "the run started with many asteroids and capital ship stand ins. but then there was a
  brief stutter and all of the asteroids and capital ships vanished from the main view, but appears to still be present in the radar
  ring. i think it happens when i reach the edge of the asteroid field but i'm not sure."
- **What:** reproduce in `bench battle 300`/`1000` and `bench typical` by flying out of the asteroid slab and past the rebase distance
  (and past 2 000 m/s for a velocity rebase, `boost`); find why the batched draws (the rock field's multimeshes, after T48 the generated
  asteroids; the far capitals' proxy hulls, M4-T23) stop drawing while their rows live on: bounds or custom AABBs not following a rebase,
  instance transforms not shifted, a visibility range, the multimesh motion-vector buffer swap (as T45 found for 2D), or the tier's
  promotion. Fix at the cause; a test that rebases (position and velocity) with rocks and far capitals in view and checks they still draw.
- **Done when:** the test; the maintainer flies out of the field without losing them (H); committed.
- **Done (code part, 2026-11-16):** not a draw, a rebase or motion vectors: the end of the bench run. `bench battle 300` measures
  for 30 s after a warm-up (about 53 s in all); at the end `BenchRunner._put_back` cleared the stand-ins, so every capital, rock and
  mine was freed in one frame (the stutter: the report written, about 45 capitals with their 400-odd turrets and 1 000 rocks' bodies
  freed), while the run's fighters stayed in play by design (the radar's contacts). Reproduced on the GPU in a scratch copy of HEAD:
  rocks and capitals drawn at 52 s, gone at 56 s, right after the `[bench]` line, with GOD and the bench's frame going in the same
  frame; with a 300 s window they stayed through `rebase 4000 0 0`, `boost 2500 0 0`, blur on, FSR 2.2, TAA, `ride` and a 35 km jump
  (`gate approach 20`), so neither the rebase nor the multimesh buffer swap is involved. Fix: the battle flies on after its window.
  `_put_back` keeps the capitals, rocks and mines (`StandinDirector.keep_after_run`): the bench-only sides' capitals and mines join
  their side's game faction as the fighters do (`BenchRunner.game_factions`; the mines' `StaticRows` go with them, the native rows
  are bound again), their guns go on, and the carriers stop swapping the run's fighters; the next run clears them as it releases the
  last run's ships. Test: `test_bench` (the battle test: after the run the capitals, the 1 000 rocks drawn and the mines are still in
  play under game factions, the weapons step natively, and the next run takes them away); on HEAD's code it fails (0 of 8 capitals,
  0 of 1 000 rocks). Captures (`references/screenshots/M7-T50 vanish/`): `b_52s`/`b_56s` (HEAD, before and after the end),
  `c_grid` (HEAD, rebase, boost, FSR 2.2, TAA, all drawn), `before_after_grid` (with the fix: drawn 10 and 20 s after the end, after
  a rebase, a boost and FSR 2.2). After the run the player is mortal again (the bench's GOD is put back) inside a fight that goes on.
- **Maintainer's decision (2026-11-16):** keep it: the battle flies on after the run, the player mortal (the reversal of M4-T07's
  clean-up is his choice).

### M7-T51 Fix: the heavy cannons' pulses leave outside the barrel and do not reach their targets

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-16, `bench battle 1000`):** "watching the big cannons fire, the bolts appear to launch from outside the
  cannon, and the bolts do not appear to make contact with the targets."
- **What:** measure on the GPU where a heavy cannon's pulse is born against its drawn barrel's muzzle at each yoke angle, and fix the
  muzzle point so the pulse leaves the barrel's mouth (M7-2, the interceptors' 1.3 m gap at close range, may share the cause: if so,
  fix both and close M7-2 in `docs/roadmap.md`). Then find why the pulses do not seem to reach their targets: true misses (lead,
  traverse T40's 6/5 deg/s, spread), hits without a visible impact flash, or pulses that end (range, lifetime) short of the target.
  Fix the cause; keep the raid balance in its band (`test_station_grid_balance`) or report the change.
- **Done when:** a test that the pulse is born within the muzzle at several yoke angles, a test for the contact cause; the
  maintainer sees the pulses leave the barrel and strike (H); committed.
- **Done (code part, 2026-11-16):** measured, then fixed where the pulse is drawn and how its hit shows; no sim change.
  - **Muzzle.** Headless (H1 alone, a still raider at azimuth/elevation 0/10, 60/30, -120/55 and a crossing one) and on the GPU
    (Forward+, RX 6950 XT, a camera beside H1), the pulse is born at the drawn muzzle: 0.00 to 0.04 m off it (the 0.04 is the drawn
    yoke one frame behind the slew), flying 0.04 to 0.12 degrees off the drawn barrel (the 0.1 degree spread). So the muzzle point
    was right. The cause was the chase view's bolt enlargement (`chase_bolt_scale`, the maintainer's 4.5) applied to the 40 m pulse
    about its centre: drawn 180 m long and 14.4 m thick, wider than the 9 x 4 m muzzle and reaching 90 m back through the gun at the
    shot. M7-2 (the interceptors' 1.3 m gap) has another cause (the muzzle placed from the lead's angles) and stays open.
  - **Contact.** Same enlargement at the other end: in the last frame before each hit the drawn head was 57 to 60 m past the raider
    (centre 30 m short), so the pulse ran on through its target and vanished. And every hit flashed 1.6 m for 0.08 s whatever
    struck, lost against the pulse and against a capital's hull (the cannons' first choice). True misses are by design: still targets
    are hit 4 to 5 of 6 (the burst's first shot leaves inside the gate before the barrel settles, about 10 m off at 1.5 km); a
    crossing fighter 2 of 6 (T40's slow traverse).
  - **Fix.** `BoltMesh.drawn_scale`: the chase enlargement never draws a bolt longer than 40 m (`MAX_ENLARGED_LENGTH_M`), so the
    heavy pulse is drawn true size and every smaller bolt exactly as before (up to 4 m at the setting's 10). `ImpactFlash.size_for`:
    a hit's flash grows with its damage past 40 (`GROW_FROM_DAMAGE`, at most 9 times), so a heavy hit flashes 7.5 times the usual
    and every other bolt, beam and stand-in missile is unchanged; `HitResolver` passes the bolt's damage. GPU after: the pulse
    leaves with its tail 20 m inside the 30 m gun and the last drawn head ends 6 to 12 m from the hit point.
    Sheet: `references/screenshots/M7-T51 heavy pulses/before_after_grid.png` (frames under `before_az20_el15/`, `after_az20_el15/`).
  - **Tests** (GUT `test_heavy_cannon`): `test_its_pulse_leaves_the_drawn_muzzle_at_every_yoke_angle` (three yoke angles: born
    within 0.25 m of the drawn muzzle, along the drawn barrel; at chase scale 4.5 drawn no wider than the muzzle and no longer than
    40 m; the size checks fail on HEAD at 14.4 m and 180 m, the born check holds on HEAD), `test_a_heavy_pulse_strikes_with_a_flash_that_can_be_seen`
    (fails on HEAD: 1.07 m against 4.8), `test_the_chase_view_still_enlarges_small_bolts`. Staged scratch copy: 872 scripts compile;
    GUT_SLOW=1 test_heavy_cannon 10, test_impact_flash 6, test_bolt_mesh_taa 7, test_bolt_rows 14, test_hit_resolver 17,
    test_hit_scale 10, test_frame_passes 12, test_muzzle_flash_pool 5, test_station_grid 9, test_friendly_fire 18,
    test_transit_scale 4, test_weapons 48, test_damage 17, test_fx_pool 22 + 2, test_standins 20, test_weapon_rows_battle 1,
    test_destruction 11, all pass. No native change, no recording moved.
  - **Balance** (GUT_BALANCE=1 `test_station_grid_balance` 3/3): raid mean 101.9 s (97.5, 106.2, 102.0; heavy 5/2, 13/5, 18/5),
    capital destroyed at 123.0, 123.5, 123.9 s (heavy 66 shots, 61 hits), the interceptors' proof 1.76 times (1 149 lost with the
    batteries, 2 028 parked; bar 1.5): T40's figures, as a drawing change should leave them.
  - Touches `src/weapons/bolt_mesh.gd` (one constant, `drawn_scale` and its use), which T52 is reshaping: T52 keeps the cap.
- **Accepted (maintainer, 2026-11-16):** heavy pulses leave the barrel and strike, live on fd45acba (with T52's tail from the muzzle).

### M7-T52 Bolts drawn as teardrops

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-16):** "a larger problem with all bolts is that they appear to be straight lines. expected appearance is
  more of a teardrop, with the tail facing behind as it moves."
- **What:** every bolt look (`BoltMesh`'s capsule per weapon, the heavy cannons' large green pulse, the interceptors' pulses, the
  stand-ins' bolts) drawn as a teardrop: a round bright head at the front, a tail tapering to a point behind it along the flight,
  its glow fading toward the tail. Check why they read as lines now: the capsule's proportions, motion blur stretching them, and the
  opaque draw under motion vectors (always on since T47 turned blur on by default). Keep one draw per look and T38's motion vectors
  and slot quarantine. A look sheet (close, mid and far, still and moving, blur on and off) goes to the maintainer before commit.
- **Done when:** the maintainer approves the sheet; a test of the shape (head wider than tail, tail behind the motion); committed.
- **Done (code part, 2026-11-16):** the maintainer picked candidate C (a dim envelope round a short bright core) for every look,
  kept the blur exemption and gave the station's light batteries and turrets orange bolts. `BoltShape` builds each look's mesh (two
  nested drops, 440 triangles, head at the bolt's place, the tail 1.4 times the look's bolt length behind it); `Projectile.bolt_mesh`
  and `projectile.tscn`'s default use it; the bolt materials carry the vertex colour, the depth write, the draw order and the
  culling (architecture §7.1, §7.5, §7.6). Under TAA the opaque envelope hid the core; the materials now draw the far walls, so the
  core's is in front, and its vertex colour equals the additive sum, so the core shows in both draws, still one draw per look.
  `grid_light_pulse` and `turret_light` have `light_battery_bolt.tres` and `turret_light_bolt.tres` in their `bolt_color`.
  Tests: `test_bolt_shape` (head wider than tail, tail behind along the flight, darker tail, core brighter than the envelope in both
  draws, the materials), `test_weapons` (the teardrop), `test_bolt_teardrop_gpu` (under TAA and SMAA the head's centre outshines
  the envelope and the tail; with back faces culled it fails). Cost, `bench battle 300` at 1920x1080 on the RX 6950 XT, one run
  each: tick median 4.26 then 4.31 ms (noise), 60 fps both; GPU 1.97 to 2.04 ms then 2.13 to 2.25 ms under SMAA, 2.20 to 2.23 ms
  then 2.27 to 2.48 ms under TAA; triangles 0.49 to 0.53 M then 0.49 to 0.56 M. Sheet: `references/screenshots/M7-T52 teardrop
  bolts/t52_final_sheet.png`. Open: the maintainer's look (H). The teardrop runs 1.4 times the look's length behind the bolt with a
  head twice its radius, so the heavy pulse (T51's 40 m cap counts `bolt_length_m`) draws 56 m with a 3.2 m head; the
  `turret_light_brakiri` bolt has no material yet and draws the Starfury's blue.
- **Fix round (2026-11-16):** drawn whole at the shot, the teardrop's tail (1.4 times the look's length behind the head) reached back
  through the gun: the heavy pulse's 53 m behind its muzzle, every fighter bolt's behind its own. T51's test checked only the
  weapon's numbers, not the drawn mesh. Now a bolt's tail is drawn no longer than it has flown since it was fired, so it grows out
  of the muzzle to its full length; the head stays the bolt's true place.
  - **Core bolts:** the bolt rows carry `flown` (the distance flown since firing, summed in `bolt_kernels::advance`);
    `instance_transforms` scales each instance along its flight by `tail_share(flown at the drawn moment, tail x enlargement)` (at
    least `MIN_TAIL_SHARE`, 0.001: a thin disc at the muzzle). `bolt_transforms` takes the look's tail (`BoltMesh.tail_m`,
    `BoltShape.tail_length`); one draw per look as before. `BoltSlotQuarantine`'s native test keeps a slot's occupant when the
    basis's two side axes are unchanged (it compared the first row, which the growing tail changes), so T38's motion vectors keep a
    growing bolt. **Node bolts** (the player's, the stand-ins' missiles): `Projectile` scales its mesh the same way per tick.
    Drawing only: the parity recordings did not move.
  - **Brakiri turret:** `turret_light_brakiri` has its own `turret_light_brakiri_bolt.tres` in its green `bolt_color`.
  - **Tests:** `test_heavy_cannon` measures the drawn mesh (instance transform times the mesh bounds) over the first three frames
    at three yoke angles: nothing behind the muzzle plane (fails on HEAD at 52.8 m); `test_bolt_rows`
    `test_a_fighter_bolts_drawn_tail_grows_from_its_muzzle` (the tail as long as the bolt has flown, 0 to full, at five fractions a
    tick) and `test_weapons` `test_a_bolts_drawn_tail_grows_out_of_the_muzzle` (the node path); `test_bolt_shape` (the Brakiri
    material); Rust `bolt_kernels::tail_tests` (growth, enlargement, the quarantine keeping a growing bolt, a turned one new).
    Staged scratch copy: 875 scripts compile; Rust 210 pass; GUT_SLOW=1 test_bolt_shape 9, test_bolt_rows 15, test_bolt_mesh_taa 7,
    test_bolt_slot_quarantine 10, test_native_constants 2, test_weapons 49, test_heavy_cannon 10, test_bolt_sync 2,
    test_sim_core 21, test_core_state 7, test_hit_scale 10, test_hit_broad_phase 8, test_weapon_rows_battle 1, test_turret_kernel 12,
    test_mine_kernel 4, test_weapon_charge 10, test_anti_aliasing 6, test_muzzle_flash_pool 5, test_station_grid 9,
    test_upscaler 10, test_parity_fixture 3, test_frame_passes 12, test_hit_resolver 17, test_standins 20, test_core_threads 7, all
    pass; with a window on the RX 6950 XT test_bolt_teardrop_gpu 2 and test_motion_vectors_gpu 5 pass.
  - **GPU (Forward+, RX 6950 XT), at 0.1 speed:** H1's pulse 3, 27, 53 and 80 m out: before, its head sits on the muzzle with the
    tail back through the gun; after, a bright disc at the muzzle, then a short drop growing from it. The Starfury's first bolt
    from the chase view: before, its tail shows below the hull behind the guns; after, nothing behind the muzzle. Sheet:
    `references/screenshots/M7-T52 teardrop bolts/tail_from_muzzle.png` (frames under `tail_frames/`). The tail's tip stays at the
    world point of the shot, so a ship flying fast forward sees it up to its own speed's share of the tail behind its moving muzzle
    for the part of the first tick before the tail is full (a fighter at 300 m/s: about 2 m for about 7 ms).
- **Accepted (maintainer, 2026-11-16):** style C teardrops, orange station bolts, tails from the muzzle, live on fd45acba.

### M7-T53 Fix: the multimesh interpolation warning

- **Why (maintainer, 2026-11-16):** `WARNING: [Physics interpolation] MultiMesh interpolation is being triggered from outside physics
  process, this might lead to issues (possibly benign).` from `src/weapons/bolt_mesh.gd` (the buffer upload) and
  `src/ship/thruster_visuals.gd` (`_upload`), seen in bench fights, the civilian escort and free flight.
- **What:** the batched draws interpolate their own instances between ticks (`Engine.get_physics_interpolation_fraction`), so
  Godot's own multimesh interpolation should be off on those nodes (or the upload moved where Godot expects it); find every
  multimesh filled from `_process` and fix them all, without losing T38's motion vectors or the bolts' slot quarantine.
- **Done when:** a test (a live run of the affected paths prints no such warning); committed.
- **Done (code part, 2026-11-16):** cause: `MultiMeshInstance3D` switches Godot's multimesh interpolation on whenever it enters the tree
  (the project interpolates physics), and any upload made outside the physics step then warns; the headless renderer has no
  multimesh storage, so only a windowed run shows it. The batched draws (bolts, plumes, muzzle flashes, far dots, flashes and hulls,
  door and airlock leaves, the mine stand-in) interpolate their own instances, so `MultiMeshUpload` (`src/rendering/multimesh_upload.gd`)
  clears the server's flag before every `set_buffer` / `set_instance_transform` (the node's own transform interpolation is untouched, so
  nothing moves differently). Windowed counts of the warning, `bench battle 300` before 3 and after 0; `play civilian_escort` and
  `play free_flight` 0 before and after (they did not show it on baseline either). `tests/unit/test_multimesh_upload.gd` (2 tests: the helper
  runs clean in the tree; no batched draw calls the server directly).

### M7-T54 Civilian escort fixes

- **Why (maintainer's M5 check m5-035, 2026-11-16):** "the first round of raiders shows up with an arrival jump point and then a
  departure jump point. is that supposed to be a carrier ship of some kind? Issues: Last round of raiders comes too soon. no "hold x
  to skip" option during the opening sequence. no health bar displayed in the hud for the civilian. All must be addressed."
- **What:** answer the jump-point question from the code (what opens the departure point and why); space the last wave (the mission
  takes 6 to 8 minutes when each wave is fought, Q40); hold X (and its keyboard twin) to skip the opening sequence, as the other
  cinematics do; the escorted civilian's hull on the HUD (a bar with its name). Keyboard parity for the skip.
- **Done when:** tests for the wave spacing, the skip and the HUD bar; the maintainer flies it (H); committed.
- **Done (code part, 2026-11-16):**
  - **The jump points:** intended. Wave one's raiders arrive `via: "carrier"` (`src/props/carrier_jumps.gd`, M6-T23): a raider
    carrier opens the arrival point, its fighters come out, then it opens a departure point and jumps out. The maintainer decided to
    keep the carrier's box stand-in look (no change).
  - **Wave spacing:** a later wave waits for the one before it to be down, a 30 s breather after that, and the civilian's progress
    mark, whichever comes last. The 50 % and 75 % marks are now "as close to NAV BETA as the point is", so a civilian back from
    fleeing never turns back for them; the route keeps only its 7.5 km and 29 km waypoints. `TriggerProximity.place_at` (optional)
    says where a forced skip puts the actor. Seed 3, each wave fought: waves jump in at 69, 179 and 304 s, are cleared at 149, 274
    and 418 s, and the mission ends at 431 s (7.2 min; 626 s before).
  - **Skip the opening:** holding X for 2 s (M7-T27's hold, keyboard twin Shift / Mouse 4 from the live bindings) skips the intro
    and the briefing to the step the script names in `opening_until()` (escort: `depart`), with their world changes, as `goto`
    does. A new `SkipPrompt` layer above the letterbox and the HUD shows `HOLD X / SHIFT / MOUSE 4: SKIP` (24 px at 1080p) and the
    hold's bar.
  - **The civilian's hull:** `m.show_hull(name)` puts a ship's name, hull % and a bar under the objectives, top right in the chase
    view and the cockpit, amber then red as it drops, `LOST` when destroyed; the escort calls it at the hand-over. Console: the
    `mission` provider's `opening_skip` and `hull_bars` (info.md).
  - **Tests:** `test_civilian_escort.gd` (the skip on the pad and the keyboard, a tap skips nothing, the HUD bar, a wave waits
    for the last one down and the breather, a detour still brings the next wave, a fought run takes 6 to 8 minutes),
    `test_mission_sequencer.gd` (the opening's skip and its checks), `test_hud_layout.gd` (the bar, `LOST`, the prompt frame).
    Screenshots `references/screenshots/M7-T54 escort HUD and skip/`. Left for the maintainer: fly it (H).

### M7-T56 Encounters pacing and civilian traffic

- **Why (maintainer's M5 check m5-056, 2026-11-16):** "Less civilian traffic in the mix than expected. didn't feel like 1-3 minutes.
  first arrival was quick but then the next raid felt more like 5+ minutes. A Saturn 3 freighter showed up as an ally rather than as a
  civilian. Civilian traffic did not appear to go from Bayblon 5 to the jumpgate and back. it instead was in strange places. needs to
  be a bit busier with civilian traffic."
- **What:** measure the encounter director's event spacing in free flight and hold it to 1 to 3 minutes; more civilian traffic,
  running between Babylon 5 and the jumpgate (arrivals through the gate to the station, departures back); the Saturn 3 freighter's
  faction (civilian, never an ally); keep raids and patrols in the mix.
- **Done when:** tests for the spacing, the routes and the factions; the maintainer flies free flight (H); committed.
- **Done (code part, 2026-11-16):**
  - **Measured (free flight, shipped pools, player invulnerable, raiders shot down 90 s after they arrive, sim time).** Before, seed 5:
    the first event (a 6-Starfury patrol) at 104 s, then nothing for 11 minutes: the next patrol waited for allies that the
    station's Alpha wing (scrambled against the station traffic's own raid at 150 s) shared out of the same 12-ship pool, and every
    later event queued behind it (each event had a fixed slot, so a held one held all). The station traffic's own clock (every
    150 s) ran beside it, so the two sources were never spaced together. After, seeds 5, 11, 23 over 16 minutes: first events at
    88, 165 and 129 s, then gaps 87, 78, 132, 162, 116, 100, 155 s; 90, 92, 90, 174, 140, 62, 135 s; 82, 167, 152, 164, 119 s (all
    within 60-180 s); raids (at the player, at a civilian and at the station), patrols and traffic mixed (seed 23 drew no raid
    in its 16 minutes). No civilian was lost to anything but raiders; 2-3 docked and 2-3 undocked and left through the gate per run.
  - **Pacing.** `EncounterPlan.gap_of(i)`: each event is due half to one and a half intervals after the last one started (1 to 3
    minutes at the default 120 s), not at an absolute slot. One that cannot start (the cap, or its pool spent) gives way to the
    same index drawn as another kind, gentlest first (`substitutes`: traffic, patrol, raid), and waits only when none fits. The
    shipped pools now come on top of what the station keeps for its raids and wings (12 raiders and 12 Starfuries each; 75 pooled
    ships, was 42). Station raids moved from the station traffic's own clock into the encounters: `station_raid_share` (0.3) sends
    a raid at Babylon 5 when the player is within 25 km of it, 11-17 km out of its centre, its wing scrambled.
  - **Civilian traffic on the run (the "strange places").** The jumpgate is 50 km from the station and the player starts 8 km from
    it, so the director never used the gate (25 km reach) and sent all its traffic on straight lines past the player from 26-30 km
    out, in any direction; the station's own docking traffic came every 150 s at best. Every ship that came from the gate to
    dock rammed the drum beside the mouth (it crossed the lane's axis at 120 m/s, overshot the first point and came back at an
    angle); and every departure vanished 5 km out: a
    route given in place of another kept the old one's end (`BehaviourFollowPath` progress, the native slot not restarted), so a ship
    sent to the gate after its lane was released at once. Now: `BehaviourFollowPath.attach` and `AiPilot` restart any new route; an
    arrival flies a lane-style route that slows into a point 3 km out on its lane's axis before taking the lane (the Brezebel, the
    Skylark and the Narn transport dock from 10 km out with no contact); arrivals keep 600 m to one side of the run and departures
    to the other, turning out of and into the gate 3 km off it on their own side, so they never meet head on; the docking bay
    takes one ship at a time (arrivals wait at their own points along its axis, 700 m apart; no undocking while one is on the
    lane), as two arrivals met at one entry point and an undocking ship met a docking one in the tunnel in the first runs; the
    station keeps **5 civilians** on the run between the gate and Babylon 5 (`data/station/traffic.json`: `lane_ships`, at least 40 s
    between its own starts), placed along it when free flight starts (arriving and leaving by turns), one more through the gate or
    undocking whenever one has docked or left; the encounters' traffic events join the run as arriving convoys (1-3 ships, 800 m
    apart, past the player's radar): civilians on the run 5-13 in the measured runs, 8 the median (5 of the run plus the convoys).
  - **The Saturn 3 "ally".** Its faction is right (Earth Alliance, the civilian role; Q24, the flee-to-own-faction rule needs it):
    the radar dot, the target box and the target preview coloured by faction relation, so an Earth Alliance civilian was drawn
    blue like a Starfury. They now take the civilian colour (`Factions.CIVILIAN_COLOR`, the target menu's) for any non-hostile
    civilian hull; the core's `radar_contacts` gives each contact's civilian flag.
  - **Found, left for its own fix:** an undocking Narn transport rams the doorway (the drum, 24 m off the axis, 25 m inside the
    inner doors) 3 s after they open and is destroyed, already on HEAD; the Skylark and the Brezebel come out clean. The run undocks only
    those two; the console's `traffic undock` still picks the Narn in its turn.
  - Tests: `test_encounter_plan` (gaps, substitutes, station share), `test_encounters` (the pace with a spent pool and with the
    shipped pools, the 75 pooled ships), `test_station_run` (the run full from the start and on the gate-station line, a departure
    flying on to the gate, a new route from its first waypoint, one refill at a time, a convoy past the radar, a station raid),
    `test_civilian_colours`, and two arrivals queueing and docking without contact. Screenshots:
    `references/screenshots/M7-T56 traffic lane/` (GPU, Forward+ at 60 fps: the run's line of civilian dots on the radar, a
    Brezebel and a Saturn targeted in the civilian colour).

### M7-T57 The station tour: "Defense" and the grid stop

- **Why (maintainer's M7 check m7-002, 2026-11-16):** "Defense is spelled with an s. not Defense. Defense grid tour stop needs to
  mirror the position so it shows the turrets and interceptors from in front rather than from behind."
- **What:** every player-visible "Defense" (tour labels, HUD, menus, console help) becomes "Defense"; code identifiers and docs may
  stay. The grid tour stop's camera moves to the guns' front side.
- **Done when:** a test (no player-visible "Defense"; the stop's camera on the guns' front side); the maintainer tours it (H); committed.
- **Done (code part, 2026-11-16):** the grid stop caption and the `station` grid console help say "Defense"; the stop id `defense_grid` and docs keep the old spelling. The stop's camera moved from z -2100 to the mirrored z -2410 (the bow side the heads' guns face), looking back at L1, L2 and the arm head (screenshots `references/screenshots/M7-T57 tour/before.png`, `after.png`). `tests/unit/test_tour_defense.gd` (3 tests). Left for the maintainer: tour it (H).
- **Fix round (code part, 2026-11-16):** the maintainer saw the first framing from the front but too poor to read. The `defense_grid` stop now stands 85 m ahead of the arm head and shows L1, L2 and the arm's interceptor battery I1 (the turrets are domes of a tenth of the frame). The heavy cannons H1, H2 and the bow tower's battery I5 are on the station's frame, 1200 m forward of the arm head, and the arm stop turns about the drum to the wing it films, so no one shot or move holds both without a camera path that crosses the frame block at z -2500: a new stop `heavy_cannons` (after the grid stop, eight stops) films them from 190 m ahead and above the bow tower. Captions "DEFENSE GRID: LIGHT TURRETS AND AN INTERCEPTOR BATTERY ON THE ARM OF WING {wing}" and "DEFENSE GRID: HEAVY CANNONS AND THE BOW TOWER'S INTERCEPTORS". Tests: `test_tour_defense.gd` checks each stop's mounts (L1, L2, I1; H1, H2, I5) are in front and inside the opening frustum, `test_tour_book.gd` (eight stops, fixture mounts, the heavy stop in frame in every shot), `test_station_tour.gd` (both stops on screen). Screenshots `references/screenshots/M7-T57 tour/after2_*.png`. Decision for the maintainer: the extra stop (the dropped `grid_tower` stop stays out).
- **Fix round 2 (code part, 2026-11-16):** the heavy cannons were small, low and in shadow: the sun (+X, a little +Z in the station's frame) leaves the bow side and the frame's top dark, so no front-side angle gets sun on the cannons' faces. The `heavy_cannons` stop now stands 130 m from them (camera (60, 455, -3600), subject (0, 402, -3490)), the cannons and the tower's battery filling the picture. Added lighting: `StationGridLights` (`src/world/station_grid_lights.gd`, built by `Station`, always on, not only in the tour) puts a warm floodlight emitter 24 m ahead of and 16 m above each of H1, H2 and I5 in the `LightRig`'s competition (the bays' lamps' way; a real light, not the hull shader's floods, which do not reach the guns' own materials), so only the guns the camera is near hold slots. Console provider `grid_lights` (info.md). Tests: `tests/integration/test_station_grid_lights.gd` (3 emitters, ahead of and above their mounts, in the rig, all three hold slots from the stop's camera); the in-frustum tests in `test_tour_defense.gd`, `test_tour_book.gd` and `test_station_tour.gd` cover the new framing. The `defense_grid` stop is unchanged. Screenshots `references/screenshots/M7-T57 tour/after3_cannons_*.png`.

### M7-T58 Wing launch: `launch wing all` and Alpha leader

- **Why (maintainer's M7 check m7-008, 2026-11-16):** "Missing command launch wing all. second issue, launching alpha wing does not have
  them respond as wingmen. expected behavior is that the rest of alpha wing treats the player as the alpha leader and responds to
  communication commands."
- **What:** `launch wing all` launches every wing; a launched Alpha wing joins the player's wing with the player as Alpha leader and
  takes the comms commands (wing orders, radial menus) as the sandbox's wingmen do. Console list and info.md updated.
- **Done when:** tests for the command and for Alpha answering a wing order after a launch; the maintainer launches and orders them
  (H); committed.
- **Done (code part, 2026-11-16):**
  - **Cause:** `LaunchDirector._station_wing` returned no wing when the player leads one of the launched wing's name ("left to the
    player"), so `launch wing alpha` sent twelve `ally_starfury` pilots that joined no wing at all: no leader, no slot, and
    `WingDirector.give_order` (keys 1-4, the comms menu, `wing order`) only reaches the player's wing's members. The player's wing
    also had only three slots (`Wing.DEFAULT_SIZE`), so it had no room for them.
  - **Fix:** a wing the player leads (Alpha in the station scenarios, the player its Alpha 1) grows to eleven slots
    (`Wing.resize`, which never cuts a flown slot) and takes the launched Starfuries as its own members, one per empty slot
    (eight beside the scenario's three wingmen), each named for its slot (`WingDirector.callsign_for`, now public) and briefed with
    the wing's order; a launch that falls through gives the slots back. The same path serves `traffic scramble alpha` and a raid's
    scramble. Beta, Delta and Zeta keep their own AI leader. `launch wing all` launches every wing, one line each (fails only if
    none went). Console help and `info.md` updated. Note: the player's wing keeps its eleven slots afterwards, so `wing replenish`
    (and a wave's replenish) can refill all eleven.
  - **Tests:** `test_wing.gd` (+1: resize), `test_launch_bay.gd` (+2: `launch wing all` launches all four wings, Alpha 8 into the
    player's wing and the others 12 under an AI leader; a launched Alpha wing sits in the player's wing with the player as leader,
    obeys FORM ON ME from the comms menu, COVER ME from the keyboard's order key and `wing order`, and once out forms on the player;
    a failed launch leaves the wing as it was).

### M7-T59 Bench capitals mortal after the run; the M4 and M6 checks made testable

- **Why (maintainer's checklist, 2026-11-16):** m4-016/017: "I haven't been able to see this occur", "Even targetting destroyer 19, i
  couldn't do any damage" (the bench's capitals are invulnerable by design); m6-094/095: "could not test this case"; m6-096: "requires
  a code check not a user check".
- **What:** once a bench run ends (T50: the battle flies on), its capitals become mortal, so a capital can be shot down and far deaths
  watched in play; check the vortex commands of m6-094/095 work as the checklist states (fix them if not) and give the maintainer a
  one-line recipe for each; verify m6-096 in code and a test (in `bench battle 1000` only capitals open points; fighters ride their
  side's capital points or the battle's gate) and record the result.
- **Done when:** tests; the m6-096 result written; the maintainer kills a capital after a run (H); committed.
- **Done (code part, 2026-11-16):**
  - Mortal capitals: `StandinDirector.keep_after_run` (called by `BenchRunner._put_back`) sets every capital's
    `damageable.invulnerable = false` when the window ends; during the run they stay invulnerable. Test: `test_bench` battle test
    asserts every capital is mortal after the run. Fighters too (fix, 2026-11-16): `_put_back` clears `invulnerable` on every pooled ship (tier hits land through `Damageable.apply_hit`, so tier rows follow; native rows hold no flag), so far fighters fight on and die as T23's flashes; the next run's `acquire` and placement set it again.
  - m4-016/017 recipe (after the `[bench]` line): `set targeting.selected "CRUISER 1"; kill` works (live headless: "CRUISER 1 destroyed").
  - m6-094: the checklist's `transit carrier point follow` was refused (`follow` is the 4th word: `transit carrier point fighter
    follow`); the recipe in `docs/playtest.md` is corrected: `transit carrier point fighter follow; wait 120; vortex collapse; wait
    30; state vortex` gives `collapses = 1`, `last_collapse = opener_destroyed`.
  - m6-095: `vortex collapse` killed the opener first, so the collapse found it dead and `collapse_kills` stayed 0 and no ship was
    ever "caught inside" (fighters at 2.5 km are too small on screen to fly the funnel). Fixed in `JumpgateHost` (`vortex collapse`
    runs `JumpPointPool.collapse` first, then kills the opener): recipe `transit arrive point capital; wait 5; vortex collapse; wait
    30; state vortex` now gives `collapse_kills = 1`. Tests: `test_bench` (the two recipes).
  - m6-096 result (checked, nothing to do by hand): `BenchRunner._open_due` calls `jumps.open_for` only for units with
    `via == VIA_OWN`, which `BattleArrivals.plan` gives only to capital units; a fighter group is `VIA_CAPITAL` (rides a capital of
    its side in its wave within `CAPITAL_NEAR_M`, `_ride_out`) or `VIA_GATE` (`_through_gate`). Tests: `test_battle_arrivals`
    (`test_fighter_groups_ride_a_near_capital...`, `bench battle 1000` plan) and `test_bench` battle arrivals test now asserts for
    a whole run: pool `opened == capitals`, `refused == 0`, `fighters_via_capital + fighters_via_gate == fighters`.

  destroyed; on HEAD before T56 too. T56's automatic traffic now undocks only Skylarks and Brezebels, but `traffic undock` still picks the
  Narn in its turn.
